denote the class posterior probability. Applying Bayes’ formula yields:

\omega^{(l)}(x)=\frac{p(G^{(l)}=0)}{p(G^{(l)}=1)}\cdot\frac{p(G^{(l)}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}=x)}{p(G^{(l)}=0|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}=x)}.

(95)

We now estimate this density ratio. First, the marginal class ratio
p(G^{(l)}=0)/p(G^{(l)}=1)
is approximated by the empirical ratio of sample sizes,
|\mathcal{A}_{l}|/N
. Second, the class posterior probabilities
p(G^{(l)}_{i}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}_{i}=x)
is estimated using a probabilistic binary classifier, such as logistic regression or random forests, trained on the combined dataset
\{(\widetilde{X}^{(l)}_{i},G^{(l)}_{i})\}_{i\in n[|\mathcal{A}_{l}|+N]}
. Denoting this the fitted probability by
\hat{p}(G^{(l)}_{i}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}_{i}=x)
, we construct
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)
as follows:

\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)=\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{l}|}{N}\cdot\frac{\hat{p}(G^{(l)}_{i}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}_{i}=x)}{1-\hat{p}(G^{(l)}_{i}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}_{i}=x)}.

Using these sample-split models, we then define the following prediction for source data:

\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})=\begin{cases}\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}_{\mathcal{A}}(X^{(l)}_{i})&\textrm{if \;$i\in\mathcal{B}_{l}$}\\
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}_{\mathcal{B}}(X^{(l)}_{i})&\textrm{if \;$i\in\mathcal{A}_{l}$}\\
\end{cases}.

(96)

This design ensures that
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(\cdot)
remains independent from the data on which the final estimator is computed, thereby enabling rigorous bias correction.

Provided the nuisance models
\widehat{f}^{(l)},\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}
defined in (
94
) and (
96
), we plug them into (
14
) to complete the construction of the DML estimator
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
under the covariate shift regime.

B.3
Theoretical Properties of DML Estimators
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}

In this section, we first discuss the general covariate shift setting by studying the properties of DML estimators
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
constructed in (
14
) of Section
3.2
. Then, we continue studying the setting without covariate shift, for which
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
is constructed following (
9
).

We introduce additional notations. For a random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
following the distribution
\mathbb{T}
, and a function
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the
\ell_{p}
norm for
g(\cdot)
on
\mathbb{T}
as
\|g\|_{\ell_{p}(\mathbb{T})}=\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{T}}[(g(X))^{p}]\right)^{1/p}
.
For the random vectors
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
i.i.d. drawn from the distribution
\mathbb{T}
, given the function
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we denote the variance matrix as:

{\bf V}_{\mathbb{T}}(g(X))=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{T}}\left[\left(g(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{T}}[g(X)]\right)\left(g(X)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{T}}[g(X)]\right)^{\intercal}\right].

and the estimated variance matrix as

\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{T}}(g(X))=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(g(X_{i})-\overline{g(X)}\right)(g(X_{i})-\overline{g(X)})^{\intercal},\quad\textrm{with $\overline{g(X)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i})$}.

We impose the following necessary conditions to establish the properties of
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l}
:

(B1)

For each source
l\in[L]
and each sample
1\leq i\leq n_{l}
, the covariate vector
X^{(l)}_{i}
is subgaussian with
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{\rm min}\left(\mathbb{E}[X^{(l)}_{i}(X^{(l)}_{i})^{\intercal}]\right)\leq\lambda_{\rm max}\left(\mathbb{E}[X^{(l)}_{i}(X^{(l)}_{i})^{\intercal}]\right)\leq\kappa_{2}^{\prime}
for two positive constants
\kappa_{1}^{\prime},\kappa_{2}^{\prime}>0
. Additionally, there exists a positive constant
c_{1}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}[(\varepsilon^{(l)}_{k,i})^{2}|X^{(l)}_{i}]>c_{1}
, where
\varepsilon^{(l)}_{k,i}:={\bf 1}(Y^{(l)}_{i}=k)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)}_{i})
, for each class label
1\leq k\leq K
, and each sample
1\leq i\leq n_{l}
. After applying the density ratio function, there exists two positive constants
c_{2},c_{3}>0
such that
\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\in[c_{2},c_{3}]
for each sample
1\leq i\leq n_{l}
.

(B2)

There exists sequences
\eta_{\omega}=\eta_{\omega}(n)
and
\eta_{f}=\eta_{f}(n)
such that, with probability at least
1-\delta_{n}
for some sequence
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
, the estimated density ratio functions
\{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}\}_{l}
and the estimated class probability functions
\{\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}\}_{l,k}
satisfy:

\max_{l\in[L]}\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\leq\eta_{\omega},\quad\max_{l\in[L],\,k\in[K]}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\leq\eta_{f},

and there exists a positive constant
C>0
such that

\left|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}{\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}-1\right|\leq C\quad\textrm{for all $1\leq i\leq n_{l}$ and for all $1\leq l\leq L$}.

Condition (B1) is a standard regularity assumption in high-dimensional statistics, ensuring that the source covariates are subgaussian, and the second moment of the noise conditional on the covariates is strictly larger than
0
. The second part imposes a boundedness condition on
\omega^{(l)}
, which is also standard in the density ratio estimation literature
(
Sugiyama et al. 2012
;
Cortes et al. 2010
;
Ma et al. 2023
)
. This condition is also consistent with the positivity (or overlap) assumption commonly invoked in causal inference
(
Rosenbaum and Rubin 1983
;
Imbens and Rubin 2015
, e.g.)
.

Condition (B2) controls the estimation errors of the nuisance components, including the density ratio functions and conditional outcome functions. We also require the pointwise boundedness of the density ratio estimate to avoid instability in reweighting. Next, we give concrete examples of
\eta_{f}
and
\eta_{\omega}
established in the literature. We begin with the sequence
\eta_{f}
. In high-dimensional sparse logistic regression models, where the conditional distribution follows
\mathbb{P}^{(l)}[Y=1|X=x]=\exp(x^{\intercal}\beta^{(l)})/(1+\exp(x^{\intercal}\beta^{(l)})
for each source
l\in[L]
, we shall establish that
\eta_{f}=C\sqrt{s_{\beta}\log d/n}
, where
s_{\beta}=\max_{l\in[L]}\|\beta^{(l)}\|_{0}
denotes the largest sparsity level across all source domains; see Section 4.4 of
Negahban et al. 2012
and Theorem 9 in
Huang and Zhang 2012
for the exact statement. In the context of random forests,
Biau 2012
shows that
\eta_{f}^{2}=C\cdot n^{-0.75/(S\log 2+0.75)}
, under the assumption that
S
out of the
d
features influence the model. For certain neural networks,
Farrell et al. 2021
demonstrates that when the true model
f^{(l)}
belongs to a Sobolev ball
\mathcal{W}^{\alpha,\infty}([-1,1]^{p})
with smoothness level
\alpha
, one may take
\eta_{f}^{2}=C\cdot(n^{-\alpha/(\alpha+d)}\log^{8}n+\log\log n/n)
.

We now turn to
\eta_{\omega}
, the error due to estimating the density ratio. In the special case of no covariate shift, i.e.
\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)\equiv 1
, for all
l\in[L]
, we simply set
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(\cdot)\equiv 1
in (
14
), yielding
\eta_{\omega}=0
.
In high-dimensional covariate shift settings, suppose the class posterior probabilities
p(G^{(l)}=1|\widetilde{X}^{(l)}=x)
, as defined in (
95
), admit the form
\exp(x^{\intercal}\gamma^{(l)})/(1+\exp(x^{\intercal}\gamma^{(l)}))
for some sparse vector
\gamma^{(l)}
, for each source
l\in[L]
. Then under standard high-dimensional logistic regression theory, we obtain the rate
\eta_{\omega}\lesssim\sqrt{{s_{\gamma}\log d}/{n}}
, where
s_{\gamma}=\max_{l\in[L]}\|\gamma^{(l)}\|_{0}
denotes the maximum sparsity level of
\gamma^{(l)}
for
l\in[L]
. Additional formulas for
\eta_{\omega}

under alternative density ratio estimation methods can be found in Section 14 of
Sugiyama et al. 2012
.

In the covariate shift regime, the following proposition establishes both the non-asymptotic and asymptotic results for
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}
, with proof provided in Section
C.8
.

Proposition 3
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2), (B1), (B2)
hold. For the covariate shift setting, there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that the DML estimators
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
defined in (
14
) satisfy: with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
,

\max_{l=1,...,L}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim t\cdot\left(\left(1+\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\eta_{\omega}\cdot\eta_{f}\cdot\sqrt{d}\right).

(97)

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
, and
\delta_{n}\to 0
is the sequence specified in Condition
(B2)
. Moreover, with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n}
,

\max_{l=1,...,L}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\log n}{n}}+d\left[(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})+(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})^{2}\right],

(98)

where
\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)},{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathbb{R}^{dK\times dK}
are given by:

\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)+\frac{1}{N}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right),

(99)

and

{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)+\frac{1}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right),

(100)

with the one-hot outcome vector
{\bf y}=\left({\bf 1}(Y=1),...,{\bf 1}(Y=K)\right)^{\intercal}
.
Therefore, if
\eta_{\omega}=o(1),\eta_{f}=o(1)
and
\eta_{\omega}\cdot\eta_{f}=o(n^{-1/2})
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
, the following inequality holds

\max_{l=1,...,L}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

In the fixed dimension regime, when
n\to\infty
, we establish that

\left[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right]^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\right)\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bf I}_{dK}),\quad\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p}0.

(101)

We now investigate the non-shift regime for which
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
is constructed in the way described in Section
3.1
. The proof is provided in Section
C.9
. Note that in the non-shift regime, the construction of
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
does not involve the nuisance components
\{\widehat{f}^{(l)},\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}\}_{l}
. Therefore the following proposition holds without assuming Condition (B2).

Proposition 4
.

Suppose Condition
{\rm(B1)}
holds. For the no covariate shift setting, there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that the estimators
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
defined in (
9
) satisfy: with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\max_{l=1,...,L}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\sqrt{d/n},

(102)

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
.
Moreover, with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}d}
,

\max_{l=1,...,L}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\log n}{n}},

where
\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)},{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathbb{R}^{dK\times dK}
are given by:

\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left({\bf y}\otimes X\right),\quad{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left({\bf y}\otimes X\right),

(103)

with the one-hot outcome vector
{\bf y}=\left({\bf 1}(Y=1),...,{\bf 1}(Y=K)\right)^{\intercal}
.
When
n\to\infty
, we establish that

\left[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right]^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\right)\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bf I}_{dK}),\quad\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p}0.

(104)

The following lemma establishes the properties for the scaled covariance matrix
n_{l}\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
. The proof is provided in Section
D.6
.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2)
and
(B1)
hold. Then there exist positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that
{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
given by (
100
) and (
103
) for the covariate shift and non-shift regimes, respectively, satisfy that

n_{l}\cdot[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}\in[c_{1},c_{2}]
for all
j\in[dK]
and for all
l\in[L]
.
Moreover, when the dimension
d
is fixed, there exists some positive constant
C>0
, such that
\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq C
.

Combining Propositions
3
,
4
and Lemma
7
, Conditions (A1), (A5) introduced in the main article hold provided that Conditions (B1) and (B2) hold.

B.4
Additional Details for Statistical Inferential Procedure

We now present the full procedures of the statistical inference in Algorithm
3
.

1

Data:

Target covariates
\{X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}}\}_{j\in[N]}
; the DML estimator
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
for
1\leq l\leq L
with estimated covariance matrix
\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}
; the resampling size
M
.

Result:

Confidence interval
{\rm CI}
.

for

1\leq m\leq M

do

Sample
\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}
following the distribution
\mathcal{N}\left(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)},\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}\right)
for
1\leq l\leq L
;
end for

Construct the filtered index set
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
).

for

m\in\mathcal{M}

do

Compute
\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}
as in (
34
) and estimate
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
as in (
37
);

Construct the interval
{\rm Int}^{[m]}
as in (
39
);

end for

Output the final CI as in (
40
).

Algorithm 3

Uncertainty Quantification Algorithm with Perturbation

In this subsection, we provide the remaining details of the implementation of the proposed inferential procedure in Section
5.3
. Specifically, we will provide procedures regarding (1) construction of inputs
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l}
and
\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l}
; and (2) the expression of
\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}
used in each individual interval
{\rm Int}^{[m]}
defined in (
39
) for
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Moreover, in Section
B.4.3
, we justify the claim in (
38
) to motivate our inferential procedure, and in Section we present the definition of
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
that appears in the expression of
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
as shown in (
44
).

B.4.1
Construction of
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)},\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}

We begin with the covariate shift setting. Given observed labeled data
\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n_{l}],l\in[L]}
from
L
source domains, and unlabeled data
\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
from the target domain, we construct the nuisance models
\{\widehat{f}^{(l)},\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
as described in Section
B.2
. We then plug these nuisance models to construct the DML estimators
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
, and their corresponding covariance matrices
\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
, as defined in (
14
) and (
99
), respectively.

In the no covariate shift setting, the construction of
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
does not require nuisance models. Instead, we directly compute
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l}
using the expression in (
9
) and their covariance matrices
\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\}_{l}
using (
103
), based solely on the labeled data
\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n_{l}],l\in[L]}
from
L
source domains.

B.4.2
Construction of
\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}

We now introduce the definition of
\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}
.
For each
m\in\mathcal{M}
, given the perturbed quantities
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]},\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}
, we construct
\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}
as

\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}:=\left[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right]^{-1}\widehat{W}^{[m]}\left[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right]^{-1},

(105)

where
\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})
denotes the Hessian of
\widehat{S}(\theta)
evaluated at
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
as defined in (
10
). Given the perturbed quantities
\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]},\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
, the inner term
\widehat{W}^{[m]}
is constructed as:

\widehat{W}^{[m]}:=\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\widehat{\gamma}_{l}^{[m]})^{2}\cdot\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{1}{N}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(p(X,\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\otimes X\right),

where
p(X,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
denotes the class probability vector defined in (
50
), and
\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
is given in (
100
) and (
103
) for the covariate shift and non-shift settings.

B.4.3
Justification of (
38
)

The following proposition presents a simplified expression of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}
, for all
m\in\mathcal{M}
, which justifies the claim in (
38
) to motivate our inferential procedure. The proof is provided in Section
C.7
. In the proof of the inferential Theorems
7
and
8
, the following Proposition
6
will provide a detailed decomposition of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}
.

Proposition 5
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)–(A5)
hold, and the value
t>0
satisfies
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})(n/d)^{1/8}
with the constant
c_{0}
specified in Condition
(A1)
. Then on the high probability event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, for all
m\in\mathcal{M}
,

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\left[{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})+(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right]+o(\sqrt{d/n})

where the event
\mathcal{G}
is defined in (
54
), the events
\mathcal{G}_{4}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
are defined in the following (
121
) and (
117
), respectively.

This proposition holds on the high probability event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, where we shall apply Lemma
3
, and the following Lemmas
12
and
10
to establish that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\alpha_{0},

where
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
is the pre-specified constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
. As for the requirements of
\sigma_{L}(\mathcal{U})
and
t
, we provide the detailed discussions following Theorem
8
.

B.4.4
Expression of
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)

We present the definition of the value
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
, which appears in the expression of
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
as shown in (
44
).
Given the pre-specified
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
used to construct the filtered index set
\mathcal{M}
as in (
36
), we define

c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d):=\min_{l\in[L]}\left\{\frac{\exp\left\{-dK\log\left(\frac{dKL}{\alpha_{0}}\right)\left(3\|{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}+4\right)\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left[\|{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\right]^{dK/2}}\right\}.

(106)

We verify the rate of
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
and
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
defined in (
44
) under the mild condition. Suppose that the rescaled covariance matrix
n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
, as defined in (
100
) and (
103
), satisfies that
c_{1}\leq\|{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq c_{2}
, for all
l\in[L]
, with some positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Then
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
defined in (
106
) can be simplified as
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)=C\left(\alpha_{0}/dKL\right)^{CdK}

where
C>0
denotes some positive constant.
Together with the expression

{\rm Vol}(dK)=\frac{\pi^{dK/2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{dK}{2}+1\right)},

we show that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
admits the form

{\rm err}_{n}(M):=C\left(d/{\alpha_{0}}\right)^{C}\left(\frac{\log n}{M}\right)^{\frac{1}{dKL}},

for some constant
C>0
.

,

, and

and

Double Machine Learning.

and

and

, respectively.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
3

As defined in (
21
), we write
\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}
as

\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\theta^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}+\widehat{Q}(\theta),\quad\textrm{with}\quad\widehat{Q}(\theta)=\widehat{S}(\theta)+\langle\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}),\theta-\widehat{\theta}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})(\theta-\widehat{\theta}),

where
\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}
is defined in (
28
).
By the optimality condition of
\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}
, we have

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-\widehat{\theta})=0.

As shown in (
23
) of Theorem
1
,
\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}=\widehat{\theta}
on the event
\mathcal{G}
, where
\mathcal{G}
is defined in (
54
). Together with the preceding equality, it implies on the event
\mathcal{G}
,

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})=0.

(107)

By the expression of
{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}
in (
27
) and
\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}=\widehat{\theta}
on the event
\mathcal{G}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}

\displaystyle=[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left[\mathcal{U}{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right]

(108)

\displaystyle=[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left[\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}-\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}\right)+\left(\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{U}}(\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}-\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap})+(\mathcal{U}-\widehat{\mathcal{U}})\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}+\left(\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)\right],

where the second equality follows from (
107
).
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\leq\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\|_{2}\cdot\left[\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\|_{2}\right].

In the proof of Proposition
1
, we have established in (
64
) that
\widehat{\theta}\in\Theta_{B}
on the event
\mathcal{G}
. Hence, by the definition of
\mathcal{G}
in (
54
), we have
\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}
and
\|\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}
,
on the event
\mathcal{G}
.
We apply these results and establish that

\|\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda_{\rm max}\left([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\right)\cdot\left(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right).

(109)

We shall continue the proof with
the following lemma, which facilitates the analysis of
\|\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
. The proof is provided in Section
D.7
.

Lemma 8
.

For a positive definite matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times L}
and a vector
b\in\mathbb{R}^{L}
, we consider the optimization problem

\gamma^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}f(\gamma),\quad\textrm{with}\;f(\gamma):=\gamma^{\intercal}A\gamma+2b^{\intercal}\gamma+c,

together with its perturbed problem,

\hat{\gamma}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\hat{f}(\gamma),\quad\textrm{with}\;\hat{f}(\gamma):=\gamma^{\intercal}\widehat{A}\gamma+2\hat{b}^{\intercal}\gamma+\hat{c}.

If
\widehat{A}\succ 0
,
then

\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\widehat{A}-A\|_{2}+\|\hat{b}-b\|_{2}}{\lambda_{\rm min}(A)}.

We now apply the above lemma to control
\|\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
, by noting that we have established in Theorem
2
that

{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}:=\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}F(\gamma)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)-\widehat{\theta}^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\gamma\right\},

and

\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}:=\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\widehat{F}(\gamma)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\gamma+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\gamma+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)-\widehat{\theta}^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\gamma\right\}.

By Lemma
8
, we establish that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\|{\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

(110)

\displaystyle+\left\|([\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}.

To facilitate the discussion, we define

\Delta_{1}=\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U},\quad\Delta_{2}=[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}-[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1},\quad\Delta_{3}=\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla S(\widehat{\theta}).

On the event
\mathcal{G}
, we have
\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\leq t\sqrt{d/n}
,
\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}
, and
\|\Delta_{3}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}
.
We now investigate the two components on the right-hand side of the above inequality (
110
). For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2}\left(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\right)^{2}+\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\|_{2}\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\left(2\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\right)

(111)

\displaystyle\lesssim t\cdot\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\sqrt{d/n}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

For the second term in the right-hand side of (
110
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|([\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

(112)

\displaystyle\leq\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta}\|_{2}\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2})\|\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\|_{2}\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2})(\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2})\|\Delta_{3}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

The combination of results in (
110
), (
111
) and (
112
) results in

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\|{\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2})t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}

\displaystyle+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})\sqrt{d\log N/N}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2})\left(t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right).

Notice that

\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\geq\lambda_{\rm min}([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1})\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U}).

Therefore, we establish that

\|{\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}}{\lambda_{\rm min}\left([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\right)\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(t\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)

Putting the above result back to (
109
), together with Lemma
3
, we establish that: with probability at least
1-N^{-c_{1}d}-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{\rm max}\left([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\right)}{\lambda_{\rm min}\left([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\right)\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\cdot\left(1+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(t\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\kappa_{1}\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\cdot\left(1+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(t\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right),

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
1
, and
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}
are positive constants given in Condition (A2).

C.2
Proof of Theorem
4

It follows from Theorems
1
and
3
that, under Conditions (A1), (A2), (A3), there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that with probability larger than
1-\exp(-c_{1}t^{2})-N^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\theta}-\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\theta}_{\rm ap}-\theta^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+\|\theta^{*}_{\rm ap}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\frac{t}{\kappa_{1}}+\frac{\kappa_{2}\cdot t}{\kappa_{1}\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(1+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]\sqrt{d/n},

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
.
Additionally, provided Condition (A4), we shall further simplify the above inequality and complete the proof of Theorem
4
.

C.3
Proof of Proposition
2

Due to Lemma
1
,
S(\theta)
is a convex function of
\theta
. Then the continuous function
\phi(\theta,\gamma)
is convex in
\theta
and concave in
\gamma
.
Therefore, given the convex compact sets
\Theta_{\rm loc}
and
\Delta^{L}
, Sion’s Minimax Theorem
(
Sion 1958
)
implies that:

\min_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\phi(\theta,\gamma)=\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\min_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\phi(\theta,\gamma).

Since
\theta^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\left[\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\phi(\theta,\gamma)\right]
and
\Theta_{\rm loc}\subset\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, it further implies that

\theta^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\phi(\theta,\gamma).

Recall that we have defined
\gamma^{*}\in\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\min_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\phi(\gamma,\theta)
as in (
41
). Then it follows from Proposition 3.4.1 of
Bertsekas 2009
,
(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*})
is a saddle point satisfying

\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma)\leq\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*})\leq\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*}),\quad\textrm{for all }\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc},\gamma\in\Delta^{L}.

The second inequality of the above equation implies that

\theta^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*}).

(113)

The above result implies that
\theta^{*}
is a local minimum of
\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*})
in the neighborhood of
\Theta_{\rm loc}
.

In the following, we show that
\theta^{*}
is also a global minimum over the entire space
\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, by the argument of contradiction. Suppose that
\theta^{*}
is not a global minimizer of
\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*})
over the entire space
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
. Together with (
113
), we obtain that there exists a point
\tilde{\theta}
such that

\phi(\tilde{\theta},\gamma^{*})<\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*}),\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tilde{\theta}\notin\Theta_{\rm loc}.

Since
\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*})
is convex towards
\theta
, for any
\nu\in[0,1)
, it holds that

\phi\left(\tilde{\theta}+\nu(\theta^{*}-\tilde{\theta}),\;\gamma^{*}\right)\leq\nu\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*})+(1-\nu)\phi(\tilde{\theta},\gamma^{*})<\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*}).

(114)

Since
\theta^{*}
is an interior point of
\Theta_{\rm loc}
, and
\tilde{\theta}\notin\Theta_{\rm loc}
, the pathway
\nu\mapsto\nu\theta^{*}+(1-\nu)\tilde{\theta}
intersects
\Theta_{\rm loc}
at the point
\theta_{\rm inter}
, which admits the following expression:

\theta_{\rm inter}=\nu\theta^{*}+(1-\nu)\tilde{\theta},\quad\textrm{with}\quad\nu=1-\frac{2\tau\sqrt{d/n}}{\|\tilde{\theta}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}}\in(0,1).

And it follows from (
114
) that
\theta_{\rm inter}
satisfies:

\phi(\theta_{\rm inter},\gamma^{*})<\phi(\theta^{*},\gamma^{*}),\quad\textrm{and}\quad\theta_{\rm inter}\in\Theta_{\rm loc},

which contradicts (
113
). Therefore, we establish

\theta^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\phi(\theta,\gamma^{*}).
Together with (
113
), we complete the proof.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
6

We start with the following lemma, which provides a upper bound for
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
via
\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}
. The proof is provided in Section
D.8
.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2)
–
(A4)
hold. On the event
\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3}
defined in (
54
), the following inequality holds for all
1\leq m\leq M
,

\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\cdot\left(\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right),

(115)

where
\kappa_{2}>0
is given in Condition
(A2)
.

By taking the minimum over all
m\in\mathcal{M}
with
\mathcal{M}\subseteq[M]
, we establish that, on the event
\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3}
,

\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\cdot\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right).

(116)

Therefore, to control
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
, it suffices to upper bound
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}^{[m]}\|_{2}
.

To facilitate the theoretical analysis, we introduce additional high probability events.
We denote the observed data by
\mathcal{O}
, consisting of the labeled source data and the unlabeled target data, that is
\mathcal{O}=\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n_{l}],l\in[L]}\bigcup\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.
We consider the following events for the data
\mathcal{O}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\{\max_{l\in[L]}\left\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{3}\right\},

(117)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\max_{l\in[L]}\max_{j\in[dK]}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}\leq 1.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}\right\},

where
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
is a pre-specified constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
).
We provide explanations on the above events: on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, the scaled estimated variance matrix
n_{l}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
is not too far away from the true
n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
; on the event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, the estimator
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}
does not deviate significantly from
\mu^{(l)}_{j}
for each
j\in[dK]
and
l\in[L]
.
The following lemma shows that
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
happens with high probability, whose proof is provided in Section
D.9
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose Condition
(A5)
holds. Then

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\alpha_{0},

where
\alpha_{0}
is a pre-specified constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
).

Leveraging the high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, the following Lemma
11
establishes the upper bound for
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}
, whose proof is provided in
D.10
.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that the resampling size
M
satisfies that

M\geq\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d){\rm Vol}(dK)\right)^{-1/dK}\cdot\log n,

(118)

where the value
c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
is defined in (
106
) within Section
B.4.4
, and
{\rm Vol}(dK)
denotes the volume of the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{dK}
.
Then the following inequality holds:

\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim{\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}\right)\geq(1-n^{-1})\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}),

(119)

where events
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
are specified in (
117
).

We now apply the above lemma to upper bound
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
via
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}
, as shown in (
116
).
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim{\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim{\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}\;\textrm{and}\;\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim{\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}\;\textrm{and}\;(\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim{\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}\;\textrm{and}\;(\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})\right)+\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\right)+\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c}),

where the last inequality follows from (
116
).
Combining the above inequality with (
119
), we establish that

\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\right)\geq(1-n^{-1})\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})-\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c}),

provided that
M
satisfies (
118
). Therefore, after we take the liminf operator with respect to
M\to\infty
, it becomes

\displaystyle\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-n^{-1})\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})-\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c})

Then we further take the liminf operator with respect to
n\to\infty
on both sides to obtain that:

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left[(1-n^{-1})\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})-\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c})\right],

For two sequences
a_{n},b_{n}
, we apply the fact that
\liminf_{n}(a_{n}b_{n})\geq\liminf_{n}a_{n}\cdot\liminf_{n}b_{n}
, together with Lemma
3
showing that
\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})^{c})\leq N^{-c_{1}d}
, to establish that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({\rm err}_{n}(M)/\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}(1-n^{-1})\cdot\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})-\lim_{n\to\infty}N^{-c_{1}d}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\alpha_{0},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
10
, which lower bounds
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})
, and from the fact that
N^{-c_{1}d}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, due to
N\gtrsim n\log N
as specified Condition (A2).

C.5
Proof of Theorems
7
and
8

Before presenting the formal proof, we first introduce several supporting propositions and lemmas. With slight abuse of notation, we redefine the local neighborhood of
\theta^{*}
as:

\Theta_{\rm loc}:=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}:\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}\right\}\quad\text{with}\;\tau\;\text{defined in}\;\eqref{eq: final rate}.

(120)

In contrast to the earlier definition of
\Theta_{\rm loc}
in (
42
), we do not restrict the radius to be exactly
2\tau
. Such a definition of
\Theta_{\rm loc}
is only used in the proof of Theorems
7
and
8
.

Next, we define the following event
\mathcal{G}_{4}
, which controls the deviation of the empirical
\widehat{S}(\theta)
from the population
S(\theta)
uniformly over the redefined local set
\Theta_{\rm loc}
:

\mathcal{G}_{4}:=\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\left\|\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}\right\}.

(121)

The following lemma shows that
\mathcal{G}_{4}
occurs with a high probability, whose proof is provided in Section
D.11
.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose Condition
(A2)
holds. There exists some positive constant
c_{1}>0
such that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{4})\geq 1-N^{-c_{1}d}.

For each index
m\in[M]
, the following proposition decomposes
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}
into a term that is independent of the perturbation procedure and another term that captures the variability induced by each perturbation. We establish the asymptotic normality of the first term and provide a high-probability bound for the second term when
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
.
The proof is given in Section
C.6
.

Proposition 6
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold. Then for each
m\in\mathcal{M}
, we decompose the estimation error of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
as:

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=\mathcal{D}^{*}+{\rm Rem}^{[m]},

(122)

where the term
\mathcal{D}^{*}
satisfies the asymptotic normality:

\left[{\bf V}^{*}\right]^{-1/2}\mathcal{D}^{*}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bf I}_{dK}),\quad\textrm{with}\quad{\bf V}^{*}=[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}W^{*}[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1},

(123)

and

W^{*}:=\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\gamma_{l}^{*})^{2}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{1}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X\right).

(124)

In addition, we assume the quantity
\tau
defined in (
31
) satisfies
\tau\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{2}}
, and the value
t
satisfies (
18
). On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}
, if
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, then we have

\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau^{2}\frac{d}{n},

(125)

where the events
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}_{4}
are defined in (
54
) and (
121
), respectively, and
\Theta_{\rm loc}
is given in (
120
).

We now provide additional clarification on the remainder bound in (
125
). On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}
, which holds with high probability as established in Lemmas
3
and
12
, the bound (
125
) applies to any index
m
whose corresponding
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
lies in
\Theta_{\rm loc}
. In such cases, the remainder term
{\rm Rem}^{[m]}
is guaranteed to satisfy the stated bound.

C.5.1
Proof of Theorem
7

We take
m^{*}
as any index such that

\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}=\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}.

By construction of the confidence interval
{\rm CI}=\bigcup_{m\in\mathcal{M}}{\rm Int}^{[m]}
, we obtain that

\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\in{\rm CI})\geq\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\in{\rm Int}^{[m^{*}]}).

(126)

Thus, to establish the coverage validity of
{\rm CI}
, it suffices to show that the interval
{\rm Int}^{[m^{*}]}
contains
\theta^{*}_{1}
with a probability larger than
1-\alpha
, where
\alpha
is the pre-specified significance level. The remainder of the proof will focus on analyzing this specific index
m^{*}
.

It follows from (
126
) that

\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\notin{\rm CI})\leq\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\notin{\rm Int}^{[m^{*}]})=\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta_{1}^{*}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}>z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right),

(127)

where the equality holds by the definition of
{\rm Int}^{[m^{*}]}
, and
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha-\alpha_{0}
with
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
being the pre-specified constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
and
\alpha\in(\alpha_{0},0.05)
as the significance level.

We next analyze the components of the target event on the right-hand side of (
127
).
According to (
122
), the estimation error
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta^{*}
can be decomposed as
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta^{*}=\mathcal{D}^{*}+{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}
. Then, for the first components for these terms, we have

\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}-\theta^{*}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}+{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}_{1},

where
\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}
and
{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}
denote the first entry of the terms
\mathcal{D}^{*}
and
{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}
, respectively.
It follows from the triangle inequality that

\left|\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}-\theta^{*}_{1}\right|=\left|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}+{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}\right|\leq\left|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}\right|+\left|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}\right|\leq\left|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}\right|+\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\right\|_{2}.

Arranging the terms and dividing by
\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}
on both sides, we obtain that

\frac{\left|\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}_{1}-\theta^{*}_{1}\right|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\leq\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}+\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}.

(128)

Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}-1\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|\sqrt{\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}-\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\right|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}=\frac{\left|\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}-{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}\right|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}+\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left|\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}-{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}\right|}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\leq\frac{\left\|\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*}\right\|_{2}}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}.

Therefore, we establish that

\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq 1-\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}.

Combining the above inequality and (
128
), we conclude that:

\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta_{1}^{*}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}>z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}_{1,1}}{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right)\leq\mathbf{P}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}+\frac{\|\sqrt{n}{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}>z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\left(1-\frac{\left\|n(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\right)\right].

Together with (
127
), we further establish that

\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\notin{\rm CI})\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}-z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\cdot\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}-\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right).

(129)

To facilitate the proof, we further introduce the following event to control the components involving
m^{*}
within (
129
):

\mathcal{E}_{3}:=\left\{\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\leq\kappa_{n,M},\;\textrm{and}\;\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\leq\eta_{n,M}\right\},

where the two sequences
\kappa_{n,M}
and
\eta_{n,M}
are defined as:

\displaystyle\kappa_{n,M}

\displaystyle:=\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\cdot n^{-3/4}

\displaystyle+\frac{n}{N}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

\displaystyle\eta_{n,M}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({{\rm err}_{n}(M)}+\sqrt{\frac{n\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\cdot n^{-1/4}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{\log N/N}.

Note that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\to 0
as
M
grows, as defined in (
44
), and
\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p}0
, as assumed in Condition (A5). Therefore, provided that
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
, we shall verify that both
\kappa_{n,M}
and
\eta_{n,M}
satisfy that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{M\to\infty}\kappa_{n,M}=0,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{M\to\infty}\eta_{n,M}=0.

(130)

The following lemma shows that the event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
happens with high probability, whose proof is provided in Section
D.13
.

Lemma 13
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)–(A5)
hold,
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
. Then it holds that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})\geq 1-\alpha_{0}.

We now continue studying the right-hand side of (
129
), by leveraging the high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}-z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\cdot\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}-\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{3}\bigcap\left\{\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}-z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\cdot\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}-\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c}\bigcap\left\{\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}-z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\cdot\frac{\left\|n\cdot(\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\right\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}-\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq(1-\kappa_{n,M})z_{\alpha^{\prime}2}-\eta_{n,M}\right)+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c}),

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{3}
. Together with (
129
), we obtain that:

\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\notin{\rm CI})\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq(1-\kappa_{n,M})z_{\alpha^{\prime}2}-\eta_{n,M}\right)-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})+1,

which implies that

\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\in{\rm CI})=1-\mathbf{P}(\theta^{*}_{1}\notin{\rm CI})\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})-\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq(1-\kappa_{n,M})z_{\alpha^{\prime}2}-\eta_{n,M}\right).

(131)

It follows from (
123
) of Proposition
6
that
\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}/\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. We apply the properties of
\kappa_{n,M},\eta_{n,M}
in (
130
) and the bounded convergence theorem to establish that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{D}^{*}_{1}|}{\sqrt{{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}\geq(1-\kappa_{n,M})z_{\alpha^{\prime}2}-\eta_{n,M}\right)=\mathbf{P}(|Z|\geq z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2})=\alpha^{\prime},

where
Z
denotes the standard gaussian random variable.

Lastly, we take the liminf operators on both sides of (
131
) to establish that:

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\theta_{1}^{*}\in{\rm CI})\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})-\alpha^{\prime}.

We then apply Lemma
13
, which controls the probability of the event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, to conclude that: if
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\theta_{1}^{*}\in{\rm CI})\geq 1-\alpha_{0}-\alpha^{\prime}=1-\alpha,

where the last inequality holds as
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha-\alpha_{0}
.

C.5.2
Proof of Theorem
8

By the construction of
{\rm CI}
, we observe that

{\bf Leng}({\rm CI})\leq 2\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\left(\left|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m]}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right|+z_{\alpha^{\prime}/2}\cdot\sqrt{\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}_{1,1}}\right).

(132)

In the following discussion, we shall control
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m]}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right|
and
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}_{1,1}}
in order.

Control of
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m]}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right|
.

We observe that

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m]}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right|\leq\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}.

The following lemma controls the error
\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
via the perturbed weight error
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
.
The proof is provided in Section
D.12
.

Lemma 14
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)
–
(A4)
hold. On the event
\mathcal{G}
defined in (
54
), there exists some constant
C>0
such that if
C\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}<1
, then

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n},

where
t>0
satisfies (
18
).

The above lemma implies that we can control
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
via
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
, if
C\sqrt{d}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}<1
for some constant
C>0
. We now investigate the perturbed weight error
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}.
The proof of the following lemma is provided in Section
D.14
.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2)
–
(A5)
hold.
On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, with the event
\mathcal{G}
defined in (
54
), and the events
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
defined in (
117
),

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4},

(133)

where the value
t
satisfies
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})(n/d)^{1/4}
, and
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
is the constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
).

As
\tau\asymp(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})})t
, when
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})(n/d)^{1/4}
, it holds that
\tau\ll(n/d)^{1/4}
. Moreover, provided that
\sigma_{L}(\mathcal{U})\gg n^{-1/4}
, in the fixed dimension scenario, we have

C\cdot\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\sqrt{d/n}\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}\ll(d/n)^{1/4}\ll 1,

for some constant
C>0
.
Therefore, the above lemma indicates that on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, the perturbed weight vectors satisfy
C\sqrt{d}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}<1
. Then together with Lemma
14
, we establish that: when
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/4}
, on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2},

\displaystyle\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n}\lesssim\left(t+\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)n^{-1/2}+\tau^{2}n^{-3/4}.

(134)

where the first inequality holds by taking the maximum over
m\in\mathcal{M}
of Lemma
14
, and the second inequality follows from (
133
) and we omit the dependence on
d
due to the fixed dimension regime.

Furthermore, if
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
such that
\tau\asymp(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})})t\ll n^{1/8}
, we have
\tau^{2}n^{-3/4}\ll n^{-1/2}
. Then, (
134
) further implies that, on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\left|\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{[m]}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right|\leq\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(t+\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

(135)

Control of
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}_{1,1}}
.
Note that

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}n\cdot\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}_{1,1}\leq\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}\|_{2}\leq\|n{\bf V}^{*}\|_{2}+\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2},

(136)

where the last inequality holds by the triangle inequality. Recall the definition of
{\bf V}^{*}
in (
123
), we have

\|n{\bf V}^{*}\|_{2}=\|[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\|_{2}^{2}\|nW^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\|_{2}^{2}\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\gamma^{*}_{l})^{2}\|n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X)\|_{2}\right\}.

According to Condition (A5) and the boundedness of
X^{\mathbb{Q}}
in Condition (A2), we establish that both
\|n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}
and
\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X)\|_{2}
are upper bounded by some positive constant. Therefore, we establish that

\|n{\bf V}^{*}\|_{2}\leq C.

(137)

The following lemma establishes the upper bound of
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
, with the proof provided in Section
D.15

Lemma 16
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2)
–
(A5)
hold, and the value
t
satisfies
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
.
On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, with the event
\mathcal{G}
defined in (

) and the events
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}

), we have

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}\leq C,

for some positive constant
C>0
.

We plug the results in (
137
) and the above lemma back to (
136
), and obtain that: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, if
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
, then

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{\widehat{{\bf V}}^{[m]}_{1,1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

(138)

Proof of Length (
48
).
We plug (
138
) and (
135
) into the upper bound of
{\bf Leng}(\rm CI)
in (

) to establish that: on the event

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left({\bf Leng}({\rm CI})\lesssim\left(t+\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})

\displaystyle=1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}^{c}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})^{c})\geq 1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}^{c})-\mathbf{P}((\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})^{c})=\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})-1,

We apply the liminf operators first with respect to

and then with respect to

on both sides. Together with Lemmas
3
,
10
that lower bound
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G})
and
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})
, respectively, this yields

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left({\bf Leng}({\rm CI})\lesssim\left(t+\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha_{0}-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}.

C.6
Proof of Proposition
6

The optimality conditions of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
and
\theta^{*}
in (
37
) and (
43
) imply that

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})=0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{U}{\gamma}^{*}+\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})=0.

We apply the above optimality conditions and obtain

\displaystyle\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})

\displaystyle=\nabla S(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})

\displaystyle=-\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}\right).

By the intermediate value theorem, there exists
\nu\in[0,1]
such that

\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})=H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*})\quad\text{with}\quad\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}=(1-\nu)\theta^{*}+\nu\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}.

We combine the above two equations and obtain

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\left[-\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}\right)\right].

In the following, we shall decompose the above expression as the dominating terms and approximation errors

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=\mathcal{D}^{*}+\text{Rem}^{[m]},

(139)

where

\mathcal{D}^{*}=-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\left(\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}({\theta}^{*})+(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right),

(140)

and
{\rm Rem}^{[m]}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\text{Rem}^{[m]}_{(j)}
with

\displaystyle\text{Rem}_{(1)}^{[m]}

\displaystyle=-\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})\right],

(141)

\displaystyle\text{Rem}_{(2)}^{[m]}

\displaystyle=\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\left[\left(\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right)\right],

\displaystyle\text{Rem}_{(3)}^{[m]}

\displaystyle=-\left(\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\right)\left(\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}({\theta}^{*})+(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right).

The following lemma establishes the limiting distribution of the dominating term
\mathcal{D}^{*}
defined in (
140
). The proof is provided in Section
D.16
.

Lemma 17
.

Suppose Condition
(A5)
holds. Then the term
\mathcal{D}^{*}
defined in (
140
) admits the following asymptotic distribution:

\mathcal{D}^{*}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}W^{*}[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right),

(142)

where the matrix
W^{*}
is defined in (
124
).

Next, we control the remainder term
{\rm Rem}^{[m]}
, with the proof provided in Section
D.17
.

Lemma 18
.

Suppose that Conditions
(A2), (A3), (A4)
hold, the value
t>0
satisfies (
18
), and
\tau
defined in (
31
) satisfies
\tau\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{2}}
. Then for any index
m
whose
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, with
\Theta_{\rm loc}
defined in (
120
), on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}
, the remainder terms satisfy:

\displaystyle\|{\rm Rem}_{(1)}^{[m]}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\|{\rm Rem}_{(2)}^{[m]}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}

\displaystyle\|{\rm Rem}_{(3)}^{[m]}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau^{2}\frac{d}{n}.

We apply the preceding lemma to establish that: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}
,

\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|{\rm Rem}^{[m]}_{(j)}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau^{2}\frac{d}{n}.

C.7
Proof of Proposition
5

It follows from (
139
) and (
141
) that for each
m\in\mathcal{M}
,

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=\mathcal{D}^{*}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}{\rm Rem}^{[m]}_{(j)}.

On the event
\mathcal{G}_{2}
defined in (
54
), we have

\|\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}\ll\sqrt{d/n},

where the last inequality holds as
N\gg n\log N
in Condition (A2). Then on the event
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, the term
\mathcal{D}^{*}
defined in (
140
) can be expressed as:

\mathcal{D}^{*}=-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\left((\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right)+o(\sqrt{d/n}).

(143)

And it follows from (
135
) that
when
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
, on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc},\quad\textrm{for all $m\in\mathcal{M}$}.

And when
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
, it holds that
\tau\asymp(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})})t\ll n^{1/8}
.
Then we apply Lemma
18
to establish that:
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\|{\rm Rem}_{(2)}^{[m]}+{\rm Rem}^{[m]}_{(3)}\|_{2}=o(\sqrt{d/n}).

(144)

And the term
{\rm Rem}^{[m]}_{(1)}
, it can be further expressed as:

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{(1)}^{[m]}

\displaystyle=-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\right)(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})

(145)

\displaystyle-\left\{[H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})]^{-1}-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right\}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})

We now study the second term on the right-hand side of (
145
).
On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, Lemma
15
implies that,

\displaystyle\left\|[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\right)(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\sqrt{d/n}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\frac{d}{n},

where the second inequality follows from the definition of
\mathcal{G}
and Lemma
15
, the third inequality holds by
\tau\ll n^{1/8}
.

We continue studying the third term on the right-hand side of (
145
).
By Lemma
2
, we establish that

\displaystyle\left\|[H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})]^{-1}-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n),

where the first inequality holds by (
52
) in Lemma
2
, the second inequality holds due to the definition of
\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}
, the third inequality holds by
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
. Then we show that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\{)[H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})]^{-1}-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right)\widehat{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|[H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})]^{-1}-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right\|_{2}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\tau\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n)\right)\left(\|\mathcal{U}\|+t\sqrt{d/n}\right)\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}\right],

where the last inequality holds due to event
\mathcal{G}
and Lemma
15
.

As we assume that
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
, we have
t\ll\sqrt{d/n}
and
t\ll n^{1/8}
and
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg n^{-1/8}
.
Together with
\|\mathcal{U}\|\leq C_{0}
in Condition (A4), we shall further simplify the preceding inequality such that:

\left\|\left([H(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]})]^{-1}-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\right)\widehat{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})\right\|_{2}=o(\sqrt{d/n}).

Putting the above results back to (
145
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{(1)}^{[m]}

\displaystyle=-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})+o(\sqrt{d/n}).

We put the above equation, (
143
) and (
144
) into (
139
) to establish that: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}=-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*})-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\left((\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right)+o(\sqrt{d/n}),

provided that
c_{0}\leq t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})n^{1/8}
.

C.8
Proof of Proposition
3

For each source
l\in[L]
, the estimation error of the proposed estimator
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
shall be decomposed as

\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}=\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}+\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)},

(146)

where the dominant terms
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)},\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
are:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[-f^{(l)}(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j})\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[-f^{(l)}(X^{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}\right],

(147)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\left(\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\cdot(f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-{\bf y}_{i}^{(l)})\right)\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}\right]

with the one-hot outcome vector
{\bf y}^{(l)}_{i}=\left({\bf 1}(Y^{(l)}_{i}=1),...,{\bf 1}(Y^{(l)}_{i}=K)\right)^{\intercal}
. The remainder terms
{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}
for
j=1,2,3,4
are defined as:

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{1}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\left(\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i}))\right)\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}\right]

(148)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\left(\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-f^{(l)}(X))\right)\otimes X\right]

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{2}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j})-f^{(l)}(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j})\right)\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-f^{(l)}(X)\right)\otimes X\right]

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{3}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\right)\cdot\left(f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-\mathbf{y}^{(l)}_{i}\right)\right]\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{4}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\right)\cdot\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\right)\right]\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}.

C.8.1
Proof of (
97
)

We first establish the convergence rates of
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
, and then discuss the remainder terms
{\rm Rem}_{j=1,...4}^{(l)}
afterwards. To facilitate discussion, we introduce the following lemma, with the proof provided in Section
D.18
.

Lemma 19
.

Let
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a subgaussian random vector, and let
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{K}
be a deterministic function independent of
X
. Suppose that for each entry
k=1,...,K
,
h_{k}(X)
is a subgaussian random variable
\ell_{2}
norm denoted as
\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}:=(\mathbb{E}[h_{k}(X)]^{2})^{1/2}
. Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots,X_{n}
be i.i.d. samples of
X
. Then, there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})\otimes X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[h(X)\otimes X]\right\|_{2}<t\cdot\max_{k=1...,K}\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\cdot\sqrt{d/n},

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
.

Control of
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}
.

For each category
k=1,...,K
, we have
f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\in[-1,1]
, with
\|f^{(l)}_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq 1
. Then we directly apply Lemma
19
to establish that: with probability at least
1-2e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\|\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\sqrt{d/N},

(149)

for any
t
larger than
c_{0}
.

Control of
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
.
We observe that
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
admits the following expression:

\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\left(\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\cdot(f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-{\bf y}_{i}^{(l)})\right)\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\left(\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right)\otimes X\right],

since the following equations hold for each category
k\in[K]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot{\bf 1}(Y=k)\cdot X]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{Y|X}}\left[{\bf 1}(Y=k)|X\right]\cdot X]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\cdot X].

Considering that
f^{(l)}_{k}(X)-{\bf 1}(Y=k)\in[-1,1]
, and
\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)
is bounded in Condition (B1), we have

\max_{k=1,...,K}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(f^{(l)}_{k}(X)-{\bf 1}(Y=k))\right]^{2}\leq C,

for some constant
C>0
. Then we apply Lemma
19
to establish that: with a probability of at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\|\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim t\sqrt{d/n_{l}},

(150)

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
.

The following lemma controls the remainder terms
{\rm Rem}_{j=1,...,4}^{(l)}
, and its proof is postponed in Section
D.19
.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose Conditions
(A2), (B1), (B2)
hold. Then there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that, for any
t\geq c_{0}
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
:

\|\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim t\cdot(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f})\sqrt{d/n_{l}}+t\cdot\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f}\sqrt{d},

(151)

where
\delta_{n}\to 0
is the sequence specified in Condition
(B2)
.

Combining the error decomposition (
146
), the control of
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}
,
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
in (
149
),(
150
), respectively, and Lemma
20
, we conclude that: with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{D}^{(l)}_{1}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{D}^{(l)}\|_{2}+\|\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\left((1+\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f})\sqrt{d/n_{l}}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f}\sqrt{d}\right),

where
t
is any value larger than
c_{0}
.
By taking the maximum over
l=1,...,L
,
we complete the proof of equation (
97
).

C.8.2
Proof of (
98
)

It follows from the expressions of
\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
and
{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
that

\displaystyle\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}={\rm Term}_{1}+{\rm Term}_{2},\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{with}

(152)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Term}_{1}:=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\left\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Term}_{2}:=\frac{1}{N}\left\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]\right\}

For the
{\rm Term}_{1}
in (
152
), it can be further decomposed into

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Term}_{1}={\rm Term}_{1,a}+{\rm Term}_{1,b},\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{with}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Term}_{1,a}:=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\left\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Term}_{1,b}:=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\left\{{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right\}

For the
{\rm Term}_{1,a}
, as we construct
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)},\widehat{f}^{(l)}
in the cross-fitting manner, which is independent from the data,
we leverage the standard concentration inequality of the empirical covariance matrix to show that: with probability at least
1-n_{l}^{-c_{1}d}

\|n_{l}\cdot{\rm Term}_{1,a}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d\log n_{l}}{n_{l}}}.

We now investigate the term
{\rm Term}_{1,b}
. For convenience, we denote the inner vectors as:

\widehat{Z}=\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\in\mathbb{R}^{dK},\quad Z=\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\in\mathbb{R}^{dK},

and write their
k
-th block, for
1\leq k\leq K
, as follows:

\widehat{Z}_{(k)}=\left[\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}_{k}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf 1}(Y=k))\right]X\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad Z_{(k)}=\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-{\bf 1}(Y=k))\right]X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

For their difference, we write

\Delta Z=\widehat{Z}-Z\in\mathbb{R}^{dK},\quad\Delta Z_{(k)}=\widehat{Z}_{(k)}-Z_{(k)}.

For each
k
, we have

\Delta Z_{(k)}=\omega^{(l)}\cdot\left[\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right)\left(f^{(l)}_{k}-{\bf 1}(Y=k)\right)+\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right)+\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right)\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right)\right]\cdot X.

As
f^{(l)}_{k}(\cdot),\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)
are bounded, we have

\|\Delta Z_{(k)}\|_{2}\leq C\cdot\|X\|_{2}\left[|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|+|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|+|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|\cdot|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|\right].

Taking the expectation on the distribution
\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}
, we obtain that:

\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta Z_{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq C\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\{\|X\|_{2}^{2}\left[|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|+|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|+|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|\cdot|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|\right]^{2}\right\}.

By the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we further establish that

\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta Z_{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}]\lesssim\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|X\|_{2}^{4}]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|+|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|+|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1|\cdot|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}|\right]^{4}}

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta Z_{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\|X\|_{2}^{4}]^{1/4}\left[\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\|_{\ell_{4}}+\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\|_{\ell_{4}}+\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\|_{\ell_{4}}\cdot\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\|_{\ell_{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\|X\|_{2}^{4}]^{1/4}\left[\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\|_{\ell_{2}}+\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}+\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\|_{\ell_{2}}\cdot\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\right],

where the second inequality holds since the
\ell_{p}
norms differ only by constants for subguassian random variables.
Moreover, as the source covariate is subgaussian with
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|_{2}^{4}]^{1/4}\asymp\sqrt{d}
, and together with Condition (B2), the following holds: with probability
1-\delta_{n}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta Z\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\sqrt{d}(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f}).

(153)

For the term
{\rm Term}_{1,b}
, by the bilinearity property of covariance operator, we have

\displaystyle n_{l}{\rm Term}_{1,b}

\displaystyle={\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(\widehat{Z})-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(Z)={\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(\Delta Z)+2{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(Z,\Delta Z).

Therefore, we have

\|n_{l}{\rm Term}_{1,b}\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta Z\|_{2}^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\|Z\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Delta Z\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Note that
\mathbb{E}\|Z\|_{2}^{2}\asymp d
due to the boundedness of
\omega^{(l)},f^{(l)}
and subgaussianity of
X^{(l)}
. Together with (
153
), we show that with probability at least
1-\delta_{n}
,

\|n_{l}{\rm Term}_{1,b}\|_{2}\lesssim d\cdot\left[(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})+(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})^{2}\right].

For the
{\rm Term}_{2}
of (
152
), we shall decompose

\displaystyle{\rm Term_{2}}={\rm Term}_{2,a}+{\rm Term}_{2,b}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\left\{\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\left\{{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]-{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[{f}^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right]\right\}.
