Similarly, by the standard concentration inequality of the covariance matrix and Condition (B2), we have with probability at least
1-N^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n}
:

\|N\cdot{\rm Term}_{2,a}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{d\log N}}{N},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\|N\cdot{\rm Term}_{2,b}\|_{2}\lesssim d(\eta_{f}+\eta_{f}^{2}).

We put the above results back to (
152
) and establish that: with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}d}-N^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n}:

\displaystyle\|n_{l}(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|n_{l}\cdot{\rm Term}_{1,a}\|_{2}+\|n_{l}\cdot{\rm Term}_{1,b}\|_{2}+\frac{n_{l}}{N}\|N\cdot{\rm Term}_{2,a}\|_{2}+\frac{n_{l}}{N}\|N\cdot{\rm Term}_{2,a}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\log n_{l}}{n_{l}}}+d\left[(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})+(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})^{2}\right]+\frac{n_{l}}{N}\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}+\frac{n_{l}}{N}d(\eta_{f}+\eta_{f}^{2}).

Considering that
N>n_{l}
, we have with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n}:

\|n_{l}(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\log n}{n}}+d\left[(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})+(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f}+\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f})^{2}\right].

(154)

Lastly, taking the maximum over
l\in[L]
, we complete the proof of (
98
).

C.8.3
Proof of (
101
)

We directly apply (
98
) to show that: as
n\to\infty
,

\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}-{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu})\|_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p}0.

For the limiting distribution of
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}
,
it follows its decomposition in (
146
) that

\sqrt{n_{l}}(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)})=\sqrt{n_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}+\sqrt{n_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}+\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}.

(155)

In the following, we establish the limiting distribution for the terms on the right-hand side of the above equation.

In the fixed dimension regime, by classic CLT, we have

\sqrt{N}\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}[f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X]\right),

and

\sqrt{n_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right).

Note that
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{(l)},\mathcal{D}_{2}^{(l)}
are independent, as defined in (
147
). By Slutsky’ theorem, we have

\sqrt{n_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{1}+\sqrt{n_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{2}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)+\frac{n_{l}}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}[f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X]\right).

(156)

Next, we utilize Lemma
20
to establish the asymptotic properties of remainder terms.
It follows from the conditions
\eta_{f}=o(1)
,
\eta_{\omega}=o(1)
and
\eta_{f}\cdot\eta_{\omega}=o(n^{-1/2})
, that with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
,

\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}\|_{2}=o(1)\cdot t,

where
\delta_{n}\to 0
is a vanishing sequence with growing
n
. We take
t=\min(\eta_{f},\eta_{\omega})
to obtain that: with
n\to\infty
,

\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}=o_{p}(1).

Together with (
155
) and (
156
), it follows from Slutsky’ theorem that

\sqrt{n_{l}}(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)})\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[{\omega}^{(l)}(X)\cdot({f}^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)+\frac{n_{l}}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}[f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X]\right).

Applying the expression of
{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
in (
100
), we show that:

\left[n_{l}{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}\right]^{-1}\left[\sqrt{n}_{l}(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)})\right]\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bf I}_{dK}).

C.9
Proof of Proposition
4

In the no covariate shift setting, as
\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}=\mathbb{Q}_{X}
, we have

\mu^{(l)}_{c}=-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\mathbb{E}^{(l)}_{Y|X}({\bf 1}(Y=c)\cdot X)=-\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}({\bf 1}(Y=c)\cdot X).

Then the estimation error of the proposed estimator
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
is expressed as:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}=-\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}{\bf y}^{(l)}_{i}\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[{\bf y}\otimes X].

We apply Lemma
19
to establish that with probability at least
1-e^{-ct^{2}}
to complete the proof of equation (
102
).
Moreover, we leverage the standard concentration inequality of the empirical covariance matrix to show that: with probability at least
1-n_{l}^{-c_{1}d}
,

\left\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d\log n_{l}/n_{l}}.

Taking the maximum over
l=1,...,L
on both sides, we then establish that: with probability at least
1-n^{-c_{1}d},

\max_{l=1,...,L}\left\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d\log n/n}.

Consequently, we conclude that
n\to\infty

\left\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\right\|_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p}0.

And classic CLT implies that

\left[n{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}\right]^{-1}[\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)})]\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bf I}_{dK}).

Appendix D
Proof of Lemmas

This section contains the proofs of supporting lemmas.
We begin with the following lemma to facilitate the multiplication of the Kronecker product. For a matrix
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K}
, we define
{\rm vec}(V)
as the column vector that stacks the columns of
V
.

Lemma 21
.

Consider the matrices
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K}
,
A\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times p}
and the vectors
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
v={\rm vec}(V)\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
. We have

v^{\intercal}\left(A\otimes XX^{\intercal}\right)v={\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VA),

(157)

and

\left|v^{\intercal}\left(A\otimes XX^{\intercal}\right)v\right|\leq\sigma_{1}(A)\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V),

(158)

where
\sigma_{1}(A)
denotes the largest singular value of
A
.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
1

We first show that
H(\theta)\succ 0
for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
under Condition (A2).
For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, we partition it into
K
subvectors
v=(v_{1}^{\intercal},v_{2}^{\intercal},...,v_{K}^{\intercal})
with each
v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then

\displaystyle v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[p_{k}(X,\theta)\cdot(X^{\intercal}v_{k})^{2}\right]-\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X,\theta)\cdot(X^{\intercal}v_{k})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[{\rm Var}_{p(X,\theta)}(X^{\intercal}v_{k}{\bf 1}_{k})\right].

The variance is non-negative and equals zero if and only if
X^{\intercal}v_{1}=X^{\intercal}v_{2}=...=X^{\intercal}v_{K}
almost surely under
p(X,\theta).

With
v\neq 0
, for
X^{\intercal}v_{1}=X^{\intercal}v_{2}=...=X^{\intercal}v_{K}
to hold almost surely, we consider two scenarios:

•

If
v_{1}=v_{2}=...=v_{K}=a
, then
v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[(a^{\intercal}X)(1-p_{0})p_{0}]
, with
p_{0}=1-\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X,\theta)
. Since
(1-p_{0})p_{0}>0
for any
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[XX^{\intercal}]\succ 0
as assumed in Condition (A2), we have
v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v>0
.

•

If
v_{i}\neq v_{j}
for some
i,j
, then
(v_{i}-v_{j})^{\intercal}X\neq 0
with positive probability. This ensures that
{\rm Var}_{p(X,\theta)}(\cdot)>0
with positive measure.

Therefore, combining the two scenarios, we find that
v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v>0
for all
v\neq 0
, implying that
H(\theta)\succ 0
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
.

Next, we move forward considering
H(\theta)
’s property for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
.
By substituting
A=D(X,\theta)
in (
157
), we obtain that for any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
,

v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VD(X,\theta)),

(159)

where the matrix
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K}
satisfies that
{\rm vec}(V)=v
. By the von Neumann trace inequality, we have

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma_{k}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V)\cdot\sigma_{K-k+1}(D(X,\theta))\leq{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VD(X,\theta))\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma_{k}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V)\cdot\sigma_{k}(D(X,\theta))

(160)

In the following, we complete the proof in two steps:

1.

Show that under Condition (A2), the matrix
c_{1}\cdot{\bf 1}\preceq D(X,\theta)\preceq c_{2}\cdot{\bf I}
for any
X
and
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, with some positive constant
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

2.

Together with (
160
), we prove that
H(\theta)
’s property for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
.

Under Condition (A2) and for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, we have

|X^{\intercal}\theta|\leq\|X\|_{\infty}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{\infty}(\|\theta^{*}\|+1),

which is bounded. Therefore, there exists some constant
c^{\prime}>0
satisfying both
p_{k}(X,\theta)\in[c^{\prime},1-c^{\prime}]
for all
k=1,...,K
and
1-\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X,\theta)\in[c^{\prime},1-c^{\prime}]
.

For any vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, we have

u^{\intercal}D(X,\theta)u=\sum_{k=1}^{K}u_{k}^{2}p_{k}(X,\theta)-\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}u_{k}p_{k}(X,\theta)\right)^{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}u_{k}p_{k}(X,\theta)\right)^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}u^{2}_{k}p_{k}(X,\theta)\right)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X,\theta)\right).

Then we apply
1-\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X,\theta)\geq c^{\prime}
and obtain

u^{\intercal}D(X,\theta)u\geq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}u^{2}_{k}p_{k}(X,\theta)\right)c^{\prime}\geq c^{\prime 2}\|u\|_{2}^{2}.

(161)

Moreover, it is evident that

u^{\intercal}D(X,\theta)u\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}u_{k}^{2}p_{k}(X,\theta)\leq(1-c^{\prime})\|u\|_{2}^{2}.

(162)

Combining (
161
) and (
162
), we establish that
\sigma_{\min}\left(D(X,\theta)\right)\geq c^{\prime 2}
and
\sigma_{\max}(D(X,\theta))\leq 1-c^{\prime}.

Together with (
160
), we further show that

c^{\prime 2}\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V)\leq{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VD(X,\theta))\leq(1-c^{\prime})\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V).

Then it follows from (
159
) that

c^{\prime 2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V)\leq v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v\leq(1-c^{\prime})\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V).

Notice that

\kappa_{1}\|v\|^{2}_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}V_{k}^{\intercal}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[XX^{\intercal}]V_{k}\leq\kappa_{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2},

where
V_{k}
is the
k
-th column of the matrix
V
that satisfies that
{\rm vec}(V)=v
, and
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}
are positive constants specified in Condition (A2). Combining the above two results, we establish that

c^{\prime 2}\kappa_{1}\|v\|^{2}\leq v^{\intercal}H(\theta)v\leq(1-c^{\prime})\kappa_{2}\|v\|^{2},

for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
2

Proof of (
53
).

Note that

\displaystyle D(X,\theta)-D(X,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(p(X,\theta))-{\rm diag}(p(X,\theta^{\prime}))+p(X,\theta^{\prime})p^{\intercal}(X,\theta^{\prime})-p(X,\theta)p^{\intercal}(X,\theta)

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(p(X,\theta))-{\rm diag}(p(X,\theta^{\prime}))+(p(X,\theta^{\prime})-p(X,\theta))p^{\intercal}(X,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle+p(X,\theta)(p(X,\theta^{\prime})-p(X,\theta))^{\intercal}+(p(X,\theta^{\prime})-p(X,\theta))(p(X,\theta^{\prime})-p(X,\theta))^{\intercal}.

Since
\|p(X,\theta)\|_{2}\leq 1
for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, we have

\left\|D(X,\theta)-D(X,\theta^{\prime})\right\|_{2}\leq 3\|p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\prime})\|_{2}+\|p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}.

(163)

By the mean value theorem, we shall decompose
p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\prime})
as follows: for some
t\in[0,1]
,

p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\prime})=D(X,\theta+t(\theta^{\prime}-\theta))\begin{pmatrix}X^{\intercal}(\theta_{1}-\theta^{\prime}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
X^{\intercal}(\theta_{K}-\theta^{\prime}_{K})\end{pmatrix}.

(164)

Together with
\|D(X,\theta)\|_{2}\leq\|p(X,\theta)\|_{2}+\|p(X,\theta)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2
, the above inequality implies

\|p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\prime})\|_{2}\leq 2\Delta(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\quad\text{with}\quad\Delta(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[X^{\intercal}(\theta_{k}-\theta^{\prime}_{k})\right]^{2}}.

(165)

We establish (
53
) by combining the above inequality and (
163
).

Proof of (
52
).

Since
H(\theta^{\prime})-H(\theta)
is a symmetric matrix, we have

\displaystyle\|H(\theta^{\prime})-H(\theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{\|v\|_{2}\leq 1}\left|v^{\intercal}\left(H(\theta^{\prime})-H(\theta)\right)v\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{\|v\|_{2}\leq 1}\left|v^{\intercal}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(D(X,\theta^{\prime})-D(X,\theta)\right)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right]v\right|.

By combining the above inequality and (
158
), we establish that, for
{\rm vec}(V)=v,

\displaystyle\|H(\theta^{\prime})-H(\theta)\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\lambda_{\max}\left(D(X,\theta^{\prime})-D(X,\theta)\right)\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)\right]

(166)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\Delta(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)+\Delta^{2}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\right]\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\Delta^{2}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)+\Delta^{4}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\right]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)]^{2}},

where the first inequality follows from (
53
) and the second inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Next, we continue upper bounding the terms in the right-hand side of the above inequality.

We apply the fact that
X
is a Sub-Gaussian random vector and establish

\frac{1}{\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\Delta^{2}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[X^{\intercal}\frac{\theta_{k}-\theta^{\prime}_{k}}{\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}}\right]^{2}}\leq CK,

\frac{1}{\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\Delta^{4}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\leq CK\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[X^{\intercal}\frac{\theta_{k}-\theta^{\prime}_{k}}{\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}}\right]^{4}}\leq CK.

Therefore,

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\Delta^{2}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)+\Delta^{4}(X,\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\right]}\leq C(\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}+\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}).

(167)

Since

{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}(X^{\intercal}V_{k})^{2},

with
V_{k}
denoting the
k
-th column of
V,
we have

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)]^{2}\leq CK\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(X^{\intercal}V_{k})^{4}\leq C,

(168)

where
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(X^{\intercal}V_{k})^{4}
is bounded due to
X
being sub-Gaussian vector and
\|V_{k}\|\leq 1
.

We establish (
52
) by plugging in the above (
167
) and (
168
) into the error bound (
166
).

D.3
Proof of Lemma
3

We denote the
\epsilon
-net of the space
\Theta_{B}
as
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}=\{\theta^{1},\cdots,\theta^{\tau},\cdots,\theta^{|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|}\}
such that, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.
Since
\Theta_{B}\subset\bar{\Theta}_{B}\coloneqq\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}:\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\leq 1\},
we have

|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq\left({C}/{\epsilon}\right)^{dK}.

Proof of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
in (
54
).
For
X_{i}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}
, we define

S(\theta,X_{i})=\log\left({1+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\exp(\theta_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i})}\right)
and then we have

\widehat{S}(\theta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}S(\theta,X_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad S(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}S(\theta,X).

Since
S(\theta,X_{i})
is bounded for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}|\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)|\geq t\right)\geq 1-|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\cdot\exp(-cNt^{2})\geq 1-\exp\left(-c(Nt^{2}-dK\log(C/\epsilon))\right).

(169)

For any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
and

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)\right)-\left(\widehat{S}(\theta^{\tau})-S(\theta^{\tau})\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[S(\theta,X_{i})-S(\theta^{\tau},X_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(S(\theta,X)-S(\theta^{\tau},X)\right)\right].

By the mean value theorem, for some
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\left|S(\theta,X_{i})-S(\theta^{\tau},X_{i})\right|=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\exp\left(X_{i}^{\intercal}(t\theta+(1-t)\theta^{\tau})\right)}{1+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\exp\left(X_{i}^{\intercal}(t\theta+(1-t)\theta^{\tau})\right)}X_{i}^{\intercal}(\theta-\theta^{\tau})\right|\leq C\sqrt{d}\epsilon.

Therefore, we have

\left|\left(\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)\right)-\left(\widehat{S}(\theta^{\tau})-S(\theta^{\tau})\right)\right|\leq C\sqrt{d}\epsilon.

Since the above inequality holds for any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, it further implies

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\left|\left(\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)\right)\right|-\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\left|\left(\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)\right)\right|\leq C\sqrt{d}\epsilon.

Together with (
169
), we choose
\epsilon=1/\sqrt{N}
and
t=C\sqrt{d\log N/N}
and obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}|\widehat{S}(\theta)-S(\theta)|\leq C\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\geq 1-N^{-c_{1}d}.

(170)

Proof of
\mathcal{G}_{3}
in (
54
).

Given a
\delta
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
of the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{Kd}
, it is known that

\|\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta})\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-2\delta-\delta^{2}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v,

provided that
1-2\delta-\delta^{2}>0
. The size of the
\delta
-net is given by:

|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}|\leq\left({C}/{\delta}\right)^{Kd}.

Then, we establish that

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\|\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta})\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-2\delta-\delta^{2}}\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v.

(171)

By the definitions of
\widehat{H}(\theta)
and
H(\theta)
, we have

v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v=v^{\intercal}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right]v.

For any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|\theta^{\tau}-\theta\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.
For
v={\rm vec}(V)
and
v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
, we apply Lemma
21
and obtain

\left|v^{\intercal}\left(D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}-D(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right)v\right|\leq\lambda_{\max}(\left(D(X_{i},\theta)-D(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\right)\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V).

It follows from (
53
) that

\displaystyle\|D(X_{i},\theta)-D(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 6\Delta(X_{i},{\theta}-\theta^{\tau})+12\Delta(X_{i},\theta-\theta^{\tau})\leq C\|X_{i}\|_{2}\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|\leq C\sqrt{Kd}\epsilon,

provided that
\epsilon\leq 1.
Moreover, it follows from (
168
) that

{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}V)\leq CK
. Therefore, we establish that

\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}\left(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta})-\widehat{H}({\theta}^{\tau})-{H}({\theta}^{\tau})\right)v\right|\leq Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

We apply the above expression and establish that, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v-\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta^{\tau}})-{H}({\theta^{\tau}}))v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}\left(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta})-\widehat{H}({\theta}^{\tau})-{H}({\theta}^{\tau})\right)v\right|\leq Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

Consequently, we obtain

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v\leq\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v+Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

(172)

It remains to control

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v

(173)

\displaystyle=\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right]v.

For a given
v
, we have

\displaystyle v^{\intercal}\left[D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right]v

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i})^{2}-\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i}\right)^{2},

where
p=p(X_{i},\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
v=[v_{1}^{\intercal},v_{2}^{\intercal},...,v_{K}^{\intercal}]^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
. Since
X_{i}
is a sub-gaussian random vector, we have each
(v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i})^{2}
being a sub-exponential random variable. Together with the fact that each
p_{k}\geq c_{0}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}\leq 1-c_{0}
; therefore
\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i})^{2}
is a sub-exponential random variable. Moreover, we have
\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i}
being sub-gaussian, which implies that
(\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}v_{k}^{\intercal}X_{i})^{2}
is a sub-exponential random variable. As a result, we establish that

v^{\intercal}D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}v
is a sub-exponential random variable, which further implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|v^{\intercal}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}D(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}X_{i}^{\intercal}\right]v\right|\geq{t}\right)\leq\exp(-cNt^{2}).

Together with (
173
), the union bound leads to

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}(\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta}))v\right|\geq t\right)\leq|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}|\cdot\exp(-cNt^{2}).

By choosing
t=\sqrt{d\left(\log(C/\epsilon)+\log(1/\delta)\right)/N}
and setting
\delta=1/4
and
\epsilon=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, we combine the results in (
171
) and (
172
) and establish that: with probability larger than
1-N^{-c_{1}d}
,

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\|\widehat{H}({\theta})-{H}({\theta})\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

(174)

Proof of
\mathcal{G}_{2}
in (
54
).

Note that

\nabla S(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(p(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}\right),\quad\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i},

thus for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{Kd}
,

\|\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)),

which further implies

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\|\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)).

(175)

For any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|\theta^{\tau}-\theta\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.
For any
v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|v^{\intercal}\left[p(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}\right]-v^{\intercal}\left[p(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\otimes X_{i}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}[p_{k}(X_{i},\theta)-p_{k}(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})]X_{i}^{\intercal}v_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|p(X_{i},\theta)-p(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}(X_{i}^{\intercal}v_{k})^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Then it follows from (
165
), we have

\displaystyle\left|v^{\intercal}\left[p(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}\right]-v^{\intercal}\left[p(X_{i},\theta^{\tau})\otimes X_{i}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq K\|X_{i}\|_{2}\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

Since the above inequality holds for any
v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
, we establish that

\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta))-v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta^{\tau})-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{\tau}))\right|\leq Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

It implies that

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta))\right|\leq\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta))\right|+Cd^{1/2}\epsilon.

(176)

Notice that for any
\theta\in\Theta_{\epsilon},v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
, we have
v^{\intercal}p(X_{i},\theta)\otimes X_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}(X_{i},\theta)X_{i}^{\intercal}v
being a sub-gaussian random variable, which implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|v^{\intercal}(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta))\right|\geq t\right)\leq\exp(-cNt^{2}).

By union bound, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|v^{\intercal}(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta))\right|\geq t\right)\leq|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}|\cdot\exp(-cNt^{2}).

By choosing
t=\sqrt{d\left(\log(C/\epsilon)+\log(1/\delta)\right)/N}
and setting
\delta=1/4
and
\epsilon=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, we combine the results in (
175
) and (
176
) and establish that: with probability larger than
1-N^{-c_{1}d}
,

\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\|\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

(177)

Proof of (
55
)

Under conditions (A1), we have: with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\sqrt{d/n}.

We then have with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
,

\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{L}\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\sqrt{Ld/n},

thus
\mathcal{G}_{0}
holds.
Together with the results in (
170
), (
177
), and (
174
), we have proved that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\cap\mathcal{G}_{1}\cap\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3})\geq 1-N^{-c_{1}d}-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
4

On the event
\mathcal{G}_{3}
, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, if
N\geq C\frac{4d\log N}{\kappa_{1}c_{1}^{2}}
, we have

\|H(\theta)-\widehat{H}(\theta)\|_{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}c_{1}^{2}}{2},

It follows from Weyl’s inequality for Hermitian matrices that

\left|\lambda_{\rm min}(H(\theta))-\lambda_{\rm min}(\widehat{H}(\theta))\right|\leq\|H(\theta)-\widehat{H}(\theta)\|_{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}c_{1}^{2}}{2}.

Then it follows from Lemma
1
that

\lambda_{\rm min}(H(\theta))\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}c_{1}^{2}}{2},\quad\textrm{for all $\theta\in\Theta_{B}$}.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
6

By definition,
\theta^{*}_{\rm ap}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\phi_{\rm ap}(\theta,\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap})
. It implies that

\phi_{\rm ap}(\theta_{\rm ap}^{*},\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap})\leq\phi_{\rm ap}(\theta,\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}),\quad\textrm{for all $\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}$},

hence we obtain the second inequality in (
75
). We now move on to establishing the first inequality in (
75
). We use the expression of
\phi_{\rm ap}(\theta,\gamma)
in (
71
) to show that:

\phi_{\rm ap}({\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap},{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap})-\phi_{\rm ap}({\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap},\gamma)=\left[{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\right]^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}-\gamma).

(178)

Hence, to establish the first inequality in (
75
), it is sufficient to establish
\left[{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\right]^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}-\gamma)\geq 0
for all
\gamma\in\Delta^{L},
which will be established in the following.

It follows from (
74
) that
\widehat{\theta}
can be expressed as:

\widehat{\theta}=\theta_{\rm ap}^{*}+[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right).

We apply the preceding expression of
\widehat{\theta}
to the expression of
F(\gamma)
in (
73
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle F(\gamma)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)+\widehat{\theta}^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\gamma

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)+\left[\theta_{\rm ap}^{*}+[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)\right]^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\gamma

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\gamma+\left(\theta^{*}_{\rm ap}+[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}\right)^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\gamma-\frac{1}{2}[\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})]^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta}).

And we compute the gradient of
F(\gamma)
, denoted as

\nabla F(\gamma)=\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\left(\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}-\gamma\right)+\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}\theta^{*}_{\rm ap}.

Recall the definition of
{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}
in (
24
), with
\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}=\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}F(\gamma)
.
Hence, for any fixed
\gamma\in\Delta^{L}
, we have
F({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap})\geq F({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}+\nu(\gamma-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}))
for all
\nu\in(0,1)
.
Since
F(\cdot)
is concave and
\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}
is the minimizer, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq F(\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap})-F(\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}+\nu(\gamma-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}))

\displaystyle\leq\langle\nabla F(\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}+\nu(\gamma-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap})),-\nu(\gamma-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap})\rangle

\displaystyle=\nu\cdot\left[{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\right]^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}-\gamma)+{\nu^{2}}\cdot(\gamma-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap})^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}(\gamma-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}),

where the last equality follows from plugging in the expression of
\nabla F.

Dividing both sides of the above inequality by
\nu>0
, we take
\nu\to 0
and establish

\left[{\theta}^{*}_{\rm ap}\right]^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}({\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}-\gamma)\geq 0.

Since the above inequality holds for all
\gamma\in\Delta^{L}
, we apply (
178
) and complete the proof of (
75
).

D.6
Proof of Lemma
7

We first establish the theoretical results in the covariate shift setting, then we move forward studying the non-shift regime.

Covariate Shift Setting.

Without loss of generality, we consider
j=1
for clarity.
In the covariate shift setting, it follows from the expression of
{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
in (
100
) that:

n_{l}\cdot[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{1,1}={\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\varepsilon_{1}^{(l)}\cdot X_{1}\right)+\frac{n_{l}}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}_{1}(X)\cdot X_{1}\right),

(179)

where
\varepsilon_{1}^{(l)}=f^{(l)}_{1}(X)-{\bf 1}(Y=1)
, and
X_{1}
denotes the first entry of the random vector
X
. Since
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{(l)}_{i}|X^{(l)}]=0
, it holds that

{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\varepsilon_{1}^{(l)}\cdot X_{1}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\varepsilon^{(l)}_{i}\cdot X^{(l)}_{1}\right]^{2}.

In Condition (B1), we consider
\omega^{(l)}(X)\in[c_{2},c_{3}]
for two positive constants
c_{2},c_{3}>0
. Therefore,

c_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}X^{(l)}_{1}]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}\cdot X^{(l)}_{1}\right]^{2}\leq c_{3}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}X^{(l)}_{1}]^{2}.

Moreover, we consider
\mathbb{E}[(\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1})^{2}|X^{(l)}]\geq c_{1}
in Condition (B1), which further yields

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}X^{(l)}_{1}]^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1})^{2}|X^{(l)}](X^{(l)}_{1})^{2}]\geq c_{1}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(X^{(l)}_{1})^{2}]\geq c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}.

Due to the definition of
\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}
, it holds that
\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}\in[-1,1]
, therefore,

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1}X^{(l)}_{1}]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[X^{(l)}_{i}]^{2}\leq\kappa_{2}^{\prime}.

We combine the preceding four (in)equalities to establish that

{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\varepsilon_{1}^{(l)}\cdot X_{1}\right)\in[c_{1}^{2}c_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}^{\prime},c_{3}^{2}\kappa_{2}^{\prime}].

(180)

Next, we study the term
{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}_{1}(X)\cdot X_{1}\right)
. It holds that

0\leq{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}_{1}(X)\cdot X_{1}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}_{1}(X)\cdot X_{1}\right)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[X_{1}]^{2}\leq\kappa_{2},

(181)

where the second equality holds since
f^{(l)}_{1}(X)\in[0,1]
, and the last equality holds due to Condition (A2) where we consider
\mathbb{E}[X^{\mathbb{Q}}(X^{\mathbb{Q}})^{\intercal}]\preceq\kappa_{2}\cdot{\bf I}_{d}
.

We plug the inequalities (
180
) and (
181
) into (
179
) to establish that:

n_{l}\cdot[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{1,1}\in[c_{1}^{2}c_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}^{\prime},\;c_{3}^{2}\kappa_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa_{2}].

In the following, we study the term
\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}
. For clarity, we denote

Z:=\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)})\cdot\left(f^{(l)}(X^{(l)})-{\bf y}^{(l)}\right)\otimes X^{(l)}\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}.

Notice that
\mathbb{E}[Z]=0
, which yields
{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(Z)=\mathbb{E}[ZZ^{\intercal}]
. Therefore,

\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}(Z)\|_{2}=\lambda_{\rm max}\left(\mathbb{E}[ZZ^{\intercal}]\right)\leq{\rm Tr}(\mathbb{E}[ZZ^{\intercal}])=\mathbb{E}[{\rm Tr}(ZZ^{\intercal})]=\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|_{2}^{2}].

For the term
\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|_{2}^{2}]
, due to
\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)\in[c_{1},c_{2}]
and
f^{(l)}_{k}(\cdot)\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|_{2}^{2}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)})\right]^{2}\cdot\left[f^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)})-{\bf y}^{(l)}_{k}\right]^{2}\cdot\left[X^{(l)}_{j}\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}c_{3}^{2}\left[X^{(l)}_{j}\right]^{2}\right]=Kc_{3}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|X^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=Kc_{3}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\rm Tr}(X^{(l)}(X^{(l)})^{\intercal})\right]=Kc_{3}^{2}{\rm Tr}\left(\mathbb{E}[X^{(l)}(X^{(l)})^{\intercal}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq dKc_{3}^{2}\kappa_{2}^{\prime}.

Therefore, we establish that

\left\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right\|_{2}\leq dKc_{3}^{2}\kappa_{2}^{\prime}.

(182)

We then study the term
{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right)
. It holds that

\left\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\|f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\|f^{(l)}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\|X\|_{2}^{2}\right].

as
\|a\otimes b\|_{2}^{2}=\|a\|_{2}^{2}\|b\|_{2}^{2}
.
Since
\sum_{k=1}^{K}f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\in[0,1]
and each
f^{(l)}_{k}(X)>0
, we obtain that
\|f^{(l)}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\|f^{(l)}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\|X\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\|X\|_{2}^{2}]={\rm Tr}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[XX^{\intercal}])\leq d\kappa_{2}.

We combine the preceding two (in)equalities to establish that

\left\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right)\right\|_{2}\leq d\kappa_{2}.

(183)

We now combine (
182
) and (
183
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle\|n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left(\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y})\right]\otimes X\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{n_{l}}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(f^{(l)}(X)\otimes X\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq dKc_{3}^{2}\kappa_{2}^{\prime}+d\kappa_{2}.

Non-shift Regime.

We consider
j=1
without loss of generality. Then it follows from the expression of
{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
in (
103
) that

n_{l}[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{1,1}={\bf V}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}({\bf 1}(Y=1)\cdot X_{1})=\mathbb{E}[(\varepsilon^{(l)}_{1})^{2}(X^{(l)}_{1})^{2}].

By Condition (B1), it holds that

c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\leq c_{1}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(X^{(l)}_{1})^{2}]\leq n_{l}[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{1,1}\leq\mathbb{E}[(X^{(l)}_{1})^{2}]\leq\kappa_{2}^{\prime}.

For the term
\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}
, it holds that:

\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bf y}^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\|X^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq d\kappa_{2}^{\prime}.

D.7
Proof of Lemma
8

For any given
\gamma\in\Delta^{L}
, we have

\nabla\hat{f}(\gamma)-\nabla f(\gamma)=2(\widehat{A}-A)\gamma+2(\hat{b}-b)^{\intercal}\gamma.

Therefore,

\|\nabla\hat{f}(\gamma)-\nabla f(\gamma)\|_{2}\leq 2\|\widehat{A}-A\|_{2}\|\gamma\|_{2}+2\|\hat{b}-b\|_{2}\leq 2(\|\widehat{A}-A\|_{2}+\|\hat{b}-b\|_{2}),

(184)

where the last inequality holds as
\|\gamma\|_{2}\leq 1
for
\gamma\in\Delta^{L}.

From the optimality condition for
\gamma^{*}
and
\hat{\gamma}
, we have

\nabla f(\gamma^{*})^{\intercal}(\gamma-\gamma^{*})\geq 0,\quad\textrm{for all $\gamma\in\Delta^{L}$},

and

\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})^{\intercal}(\gamma-\hat{\gamma})\geq 0\quad\textrm{for all $\gamma\in\Delta^{L}$}.

Choose
\gamma=\hat{\gamma}
in the first inequality and
\gamma=\gamma^{*}
in the second, we shall establish that

\nabla f(\gamma^{*})^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})\geq 0,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})^{\intercal}(\gamma^{*}-\hat{\gamma})\geq 0.

Adding the above two inequalities, we obtain that

\left(\nabla f(\gamma^{*})-\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})\geq 0.

Therefore,

\left(\nabla f(\gamma^{*})-\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})+\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})\geq 0.

It implies that

\left(\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})\geq\left(\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla f(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}).

By the strong convexity of
f(\gamma)
, or the strong monotonicity of
\nabla f(\gamma)
, we have

\left(\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla f(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})\geq 2\lambda_{\rm min}(A)\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the above two inequalities and (
184
), we establish that

\displaystyle 2\lambda_{\rm min}(A)\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})\right)^{\intercal}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla f(\hat{\gamma})-\nabla\hat{f}(\hat{\gamma})\right\|_{2}\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\|\widehat{A}-A\|_{2}+\|\hat{b}-b\|_{2})\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}.

Dividing both sides by
\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
, we obtain

\|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\widehat{A}-A\|_{2}+\|\hat{b}-b\|_{2}}{\lambda_{\rm min}(A)}.

D.8
Proof of Lemma
9

Recall that
\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}
and
\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}
, as defined in (
34
), admit the following expressions:

\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}:=\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\widehat{F}^{[m]}(\gamma)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}\gamma+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}\gamma+\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})\right)-\widehat{\theta}^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}\gamma\right\},

and

\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}:=\mathop{\arg\max}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}F(\gamma)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\left(\mathcal{U}\gamma+\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\right)-\widehat{\theta}^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\gamma\right\}.

We apply Lemma
8
upper bound the distance
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
, which admits the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

(185)

\displaystyle+\left\|([\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}.

We denote

\Delta_{1}=\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U},\quad\Delta_{2}=\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})^{-1}-H(\widehat{\theta})^{-1},\quad\Delta_{3}=\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\nabla S(\widehat{\theta}).

On the event
\mathcal{G}_{2}\cap\mathcal{G}_{3}
, we have
\|\Delta_{2}\|\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}
, and
\|\Delta_{3}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}
.
Then for the first term on the right-hand side of the above inequality (
185
) we establish that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]\intercal}[\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

(186)

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2}\left(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\right)^{2}+\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\|_{2}\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\left(2\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

For the second term of (
185
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|([\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta})^{\intercal}\mathcal{U}\right\|_{2}

(187)

\displaystyle\leq\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})-\widehat{\theta}\|_{2}\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2}+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2})\|\nabla S(\widehat{\theta})\|_{2}\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{1}\|_{2})(\|[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\|_{2}+\|\Delta_{2}\|_{2})\|\Delta_{3}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

The combination of results in (
185
), (
186
) and (
187
) results in

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\|{\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\theta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2})\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\|\nabla S(\theta^{*})\|_{2})\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

Under Conditions (A2),(A4), we shall further simplify the above inequality:

\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\|{\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm ap}}-{\gamma}^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

Notice that

\lambda_{\rm min}(\mathcal{U}^{\intercal}[H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1}\mathcal{U})\geq\lambda_{\rm min}([H(\widehat{\theta})]^{-1})\cdot\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\geq C\cdot\frac{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}{\kappa_{2}}>0,

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
1
,
\kappa_{2}>0
is a constant given in Condition (A2), and the last inequality holds due to Condition (A3).
The combination of the above two results establishes (
115
).

D.9
Proof of Lemma
10

For the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, by Condition (A5), we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{1}\right)=1.

(188)

In the following, we continue studying the event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
.
According to Condition (A5), we have

\frac{\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1),\quad\textrm{for all $1\leq j\leq dK$, and all $1\leq l\leq L$}.

Applying the Bonferroni inequality over all
dK
components and
L
sources yields

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\right)\geq 1-\alpha_{0},\quad\textrm{where}\quad\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}:=\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}}}\leq z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}\right\}

(189)

Note that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+\frac{1}{n_{l}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+\frac{2}{3n_{l}}}}\leq\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}}}.

We take the maximum over
l\in[L]
and
j\in[dK]
on both sides, and establish that: on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+\frac{1}{n_{l}}}}\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}}}\leq z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}.

Consequently, we have shown that
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which implies that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})-1.

Together with (
188
) and (
189
), we have

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\alpha_{0}.

For the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, it holds that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})-1,

which yields that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})+\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})-1\geq 1-\alpha_{0}

D.10
Proof of Lemma
11

We observe that

\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{L}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)}\|_{2}.

As we set
n=\min_{l\in[L]}n_{l}
, it holds that

\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{L}\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}.

Therefore, to prove (
119
), it suffices to show that

\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\right)\geq(1-n^{-1})\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}).

(190)

We denote the observed data by
\mathcal{O}
, consisting of the labeled source data and the unlabeled target data, that is
\mathcal{O}=\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n_{l}],l\in[L]}\bigcup\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\}_{j\in[N]}
.
Now, we lower bound the targeted probability in (
190
) as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\right)

(191)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{O}}\left[\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{O}}\left[\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right],

where
\mathbf{P}(\cdot\mid\mathcal{O})
denotes the conditional probability given the observed data
\mathcal{O}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{O}}
denotes the expectation taken with respect to the observed data
\mathcal{O}
.

Next, we shall show that the rightmost term in (
191
) can be further simplified.
For
m\notin\mathcal{M}
, the definition of
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
) implies the existence of some
1\leq l\leq L
and
1\leq j\leq Kd
satisfying:

\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}>1.1\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)}.

(192)

Then on the event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}+(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j})\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}\geq\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}-\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}>0.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)},

where the last inequality follows from (
192
) and the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
.
Therefore, we establish that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

{\left|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j})\right|}>0.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)}\cdot{\sqrt{n_{l}\cdot[\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}+1}}.

Moreover, conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, since
\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq 1/3,
we further establish that

\displaystyle{\left|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle>0.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)}\cdot{\sqrt{n_{l}\cdot[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}+\frac{2}{3}}}>0.04\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)},

where the last inequality holds since
\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\cdot 0.05>0.04
and
[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}\geq 0
.
Therefore, conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have

\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\geq\left|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j})\right|\geq 0.04\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)}.

(193)

Recall the definition of
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
, which goes to
0
as
M
grows. Thus, there exists a positive integer
M_{0}>0
such that for
M\geq M_{0}
,
{\rm err}_{n}(M)<0.04\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(KdL)}
. Together with (
193
), we show that on the event
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, for any
M\geq M_{0}
, the following inequality holds:

\min_{m\notin\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}>{\rm err}_{n}(M),

which further implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

In the following analysis, we consider the resampling size
M
that is larger than
M_{0}
.
Together with (
191
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\right)

(194)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{O}}\left[\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right],

Now, we study the inner term of the right-hand side of (
194
).
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\geq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\prod_{1\leq m\leq M}\left[1-\mathbf{P}\left(\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\right],

where the second equality follows from the conditional independence of
\{\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}\}_{1\leq m\leq M}
, for each
l\in[L]
, given the data
\mathcal{O}
. Since
1-x\leq e^{-x}
, we further lower bound the above expression as:

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\prod_{1\leq m\leq M}\exp\left[-\mathbf{P}\left(\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\right].

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

(195)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\right]\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\prod_{l\in[L]}\mathbf{P}\left(\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right],

where the last equality holds because
\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}
and
\widehat{\mu}^{(l^{\prime},m)}
are independent conditional on the observed data
\mathcal{O}
for any pair
(l,l^{\prime})\in[L].

We continue investigating the term

\mathbf{P}\left(\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Notice that

\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})=\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)})+\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)}).

For convenience, we denote

Z^{(l,m)}:=\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}),\quad\textrm{and}\quad{Z}^{(l)}:=\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\mu^{(l)}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}).

Given the data
\mathcal{O}
, our sampling strategy ensures that the random vector
\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)})
follows the distribution of
\mathcal{N}(0,{n_{l}}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+{\bf I})
. Therefore, the density of
Z^{(l,m)}
is

p\left(Z^{(l,m)}=z\mid\mathcal{O}\right)=\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2}z^{\intercal}(n_{l}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+{\bf I})^{-1}z)}{\sqrt{2\pi\cdot{\rm det}(n_{l}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+{\bf I})}}.

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, the following inequalities hold for all
l\in[L]
:

{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{4}{3}{\bf I}\succeq{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+{\bf I}\succeq{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}C_{0}{\bf I},

(196)

which implies that

p\left(Z^{(l,m)}=z\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\geq\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2}z^{\intercal}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})^{-1}z)}{\sqrt{2\pi\cdot{\rm det}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{4}{3}{\bf I})}}=:g^{(l)}(z).

(197)

Therefore, we establish that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
,

p\left(Z^{(l,m)}=z\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\}}\geq g^{(l)}(z)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\}}.

(198)

We emphasize here that for the given data
\mathcal{O}
,
\{Z^{(l)}\}_{l\in[L]}
are fixed, while
\{Z^{(l,m)}\}_{l\in[L],m\in[M]}
are random due to the randomness of sampling. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O})\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(\|Z^{(l,m)}-Z^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left[\int p\left(Z^{(l,m)}=z\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-Z^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Together with (
198
), we further show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O})\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

(199)

\displaystyle\geq\left[\int g^{(l)}(z)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-Z^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left[\int g^{(l)}\left(Z^{(l)}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-Z^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err_{n}(M)\}}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\left[\int\left[g^{(l)}(z)-g^{(l)}\left(Z^{(l)}\right)\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-Z^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=:{\bf(I_{1})}+{\bf(I_{2})}.

Next, we further lower bound the two components
{\bf(I_{1})}
and
{\bf(I_{2})}
in the most right-hand side of the above inequality.

Control of
{\bf(I_{1})}
.
On the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{Z}^{(l)\intercal}(\sqrt{n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})^{-1}{Z}^{(l)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{dK\cdot\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}({Z}^{(l)}_{j})^{2}}{\lambda_{\rm min}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{2}{3}}

(200)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{dK}{2}\frac{\max_{j\in[dK]}\{{n_{l}}[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1\}\cdot(1.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)})^{2}}{\lambda_{\rm min}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{dK(1.05)^{2}z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}^{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{\rm max}(n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{4}{3}}{\lambda_{\rm min}(n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{2}{3}},

where the second inequality holds due to
\mathcal{E}_{2}
. We further simplify the above inequality by controlling the term
z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}
, and the third inequality holds due to (
196
). Since the standard normal distributed
Z
satisfies
\mathbf{P}(Z\geq t)\leq e^{-t^{2}/2}
for any
t\geq 0
, we set
t=\sqrt{2\log(dKL/\alpha_{0})}
and obtain,

\mathbf{P}\bigl(Z\geq\sqrt{2\log(dKL/\alpha_{0})}\bigr)\;\leq\;\exp\bigl(-\,\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 2\log(dKL/\alpha_{0})\bigr)\;=\;\frac{\alpha_{0}}{dKL}.

Hence, by the definition of the
z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}
, we have

z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}\leq\sqrt{2\log(dKL/\alpha_{0})}.

Therefore, it follows from (
200
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{Z}^{(l)\intercal}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})^{-1}{Z}^{(l)}

\displaystyle\leq{dK}\log\left(\frac{dKL}{\alpha_{0}}\right)\left(3\cdot\lambda_{\rm max}(n_{l}{\bf V}^{(l)})+4\right).

(201)

Moreover, we have

\sqrt{2\pi\cdot{\rm det}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{4}{3}{\bf I})}=\sqrt{2\pi}\prod_{j=1}^{dK}[\lambda_{j}(n_{l}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+4/3]^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{2\pi}\left[\cdot\lambda_{\rm max}(n_{l}{\bf V}^{(l)})+\frac{4}{3}\right]^{dK/2}.

Then together with (
201
), we obtain:

g^{(l)}\left({Z}^{(l)}\right)\geq\min_{l\in[L]}\left\{\frac{\exp\left\{-dK\log\left(\frac{dKL}{\alpha_{0}}\right)\left(3\lambda_{\rm max}({n_{l}}\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+4\right)\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left[\lambda_{\rm max}({n_{l}}\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{4}{3}\right]^{dK/2}}\right\}=:c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d).

Therefore, we control
{\bf(I_{1})}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\bf(I_{1})}

\displaystyle=\left[\int g^{(l)}\left({Z}^{(l)}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

(202)

\displaystyle\geq c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot\left[\int{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err}_{n}(M)]^{dK}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}},

where
{\rm Vol}(dK)
denotes the volume of the unit ball in
dK
-dimension.

Control of
{\bf(I_{2})}
.

By the Mean Value Theorem, there exists
t\in(0,1)
such that

g^{(l)}(z)-g^{(l)}\left({Z}^{(l)}\right)=[\nabla g^{(l)}({Z}^{(l)}+t(z-{Z}^{(l)}))]^{\intercal}(z-{Z}^{(l)}).

We denote
w={Z}^{(l)}+t(z-{Z}^{(l)})
and
\nabla g^{(l)}(w)
as expressed:

\nabla g^{(l)}(w)=-\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2}w^{\intercal}({n_{l}}{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})^{-1}w)}{\sqrt{2\pi\cdot{\rm det}({n_{l}}{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}+\frac{4}{3}{\bf I})}}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})^{-1}w.

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

|g^{(l)}(z)-g^{(l)}({Z}^{(l)})|\leq\|\nabla g^{(l)}(w)\|_{2}\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}.

Since
\lambda_{\rm min}({n_{l}}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}{\bf I})\geq\frac{2}{3}
, then
\|\nabla g^{(l)}(w)\|_{2}
is bounded from above, and thus

|g^{(l)}(z)-g^{(l)}({Z}^{(l)})|\leq C\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}
for a positive constant
C>0
. Then we establish that

\displaystyle\left|\left[\int\left[g^{(l)}(z)-g^{(l)}({Z}^{(l)})\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\right]\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right|

(203)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot{\rm err}_{n}(M)\cdot\int{\bf 1}_{\{\|z-{Z}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\}}dz\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle=C\cdot{\rm err}_{n}(M)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err_{n}}(M)]^{dK}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Since
{\rm err}_{n}(M)
decreases with
M
and
\lim_{M\to\infty}{\rm err}_{n}(M)=0
, there exists a positive integer
M_{1}
such that for
M\geq M_{1}
, we have
C\cdot{\rm err}_{n}(M)\leq\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)
.
Therefore, for any
M\geq M_{1}
, we control
{\bf(I_{2})}
as:

\left|{\bf(I_{2})}\right|<\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err_{n}}(M)]^{dK}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Together with (
199
), (
202
), and (
203
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O})\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err}_{n}(M)]^{dK}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Putting the above inequality to (
195
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\mid\mathcal{O}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err}_{n}(M)]^{dK}\right)^{L}\right]\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}}.

Together with (
194
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\exp\left[-M\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)\cdot{\rm Vol}(dK)\cdot[{\rm err}_{n}(M)]^{dK}\right)^{L}\right]\right)\cdot\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}).

We set

\displaystyle{\rm err}_{n}(M)=\left(\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d){\rm Vol}(dK)\right)^{-\frac{1}{dK}}\left(\frac{\log n}{M}\right)^{\frac{1}{dKL}},

then for any
M\geq\max(M_{0},M_{1})
,

\mathbf{P}\left(\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\max_{l\in[L]}\|\sqrt{n_{l}}\cdot(\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}-\mu^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq{\rm err}_{n}(M)\right)\geq(1-n^{-1})\cdot\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}).

Now, we verify when
M\geq\max(M_{0},M_{1})
. For
M_{0}
, we need
{\rm err}_{n}(M)<0.04\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}
for any
M\geq M_{0}
. And for any
M_{1}
, we need
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\leq c\cdot c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d)=c\cdot(\alpha_{0}/dKL)^{CdK}
. Since both the values of
z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}
and
(\alpha_{0}/dKL)^{CdK}
are increasing with
d
, it suffices to let

{\rm err}_{n}(M)\leq c\cdot\min\left\{0.04z_{\alpha_{0}},\alpha_{0}\right\}.

As we set
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01)
, we must have
z_{\alpha_{0}}\geq 2
, and
\min\left\{0.04z_{\alpha_{0}},\alpha_{0}\right\}=\alpha_{0}
. Thus, the preceding inequality holds if

M\geq\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}c^{*}(\alpha_{0},d){\rm Vol}(dK)\right)^{-1/dK}\cdot\log n.

D.11
Proof of Lemma
12

It follows the expression of
\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)
and
\nabla S(\theta)
that

\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[\left(p(X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}},\theta)-p(X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}},\theta^{*})\right)\otimes X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}}\right]

and

\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta^{*})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[\left(p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{*})\right)\otimes X].

For any
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, we define

Z_{j}(\theta):=\left[\left(p(X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}},\theta)-p(X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}},\theta^{*})\right)\otimes X_{j}^{\mathbb{Q}}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[\left(p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{*})\right)\otimes X],

then we have

\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}(\theta),

(204)

with
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[Z_{j}(\theta)]=0
.

We denote the
\epsilon
-net of the space
\Theta_{\rm loc}
as
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}=\{\theta^{1},\cdots,\theta^{\tau},\cdots,\theta^{|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|}\}
such that, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, there exists
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
. By the standard result in high-dimensional geometry, there exists an
\epsilon
-net of
\Theta_{\rm loc}
(which has a radius
C\cdot\tau\sqrt{d/n}
, for some constant
C>0
) satisfying that

|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq\left(C\cdot\frac{\tau\sqrt{d/n}}{\epsilon}\right)^{dK}.

By setting
\epsilon=\tau\cdot\sqrt{d/n}\cdot N^{-1}
, then we have

|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq CN^{dK},

for some constant
C>0
.

Now, we study the property for each fixed
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
.
It follows from (
165
) that

\|p(X,\theta^{\tau})-p(X,\theta^{*})\|_{2}\leq 2\Delta(X,\theta^{\tau}-\theta^{*})\quad\text{with}\quad\Delta(X,\theta^{\tau}-\theta^{*})=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[X^{\intercal}(\theta^{\tau}_{k}-\theta^{*}_{k})\right]^{2}}.

Therefore, for each category
k\in[K]
, the function
\theta\mapsto p_{k}(x,\theta)
is Lipschitz with

\left|p_{k}(X;\theta^{\tau})-p_{k}(X;\theta^{*})\right|\lesssim\|X\|_{2}\|\theta^{\tau}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}\|X\|_{2},

Then

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\left|p_{k}(X,\theta^{\tau})-p_{k}(X,\theta^{*})\right|\right]\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}[\|X\|_{2}]\leq C\tau d/\sqrt{n},

where the last inequality holds due to the covariate
X
being assumed to be subgaussian. Then we apply the result in Lemma
19
, and establish that with probability at least
1-N^{-c_{1}d}
:

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\lesssim(\tau d/\sqrt{n})\cdot\sqrt{d\log N/N}=\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}.

Applying the union bound over all
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
, we have with probability at least
1-N^{-c_{1}d+Kd}
,

\max_{\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}.

(205)

Now, we continue exploring for each
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
.
For any
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, we pick the
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
that is closest to
\theta
, which satisfies that
\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon=\tau\sqrt{d/n}N^{-1}
.
We observe that:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(Z_{j}(\theta)-Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[\left(p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta)-p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta^{\tau})\right)\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\left(p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\tau})\right)\otimes X\right].

It follows that:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(Z_{j}(\theta)-Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\left(p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta)-p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta^{\tau})\right)\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\right\|+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\left\|\left(p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\tau})\right)\otimes X\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta)-p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\left\|X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\right\|_{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[\left\|p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\|X\|_{2}\right],

where the last equality holds as
\|u\otimes v\|_{2}=\|u\|_{2}\|v\|_{2}
for two vectors
u,v
. We now apply (
165
) to obtain that:

\|p(X,\theta)-p(X,\theta^{\tau})\|_{2}\lesssim\|X\|_{2}\|\theta-\theta^{\tau}\|_{2}\leq\tau\sqrt{d/n}N^{-1}\|X\|_{2}.

Combining the preceding two results, we establish that:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(Z_{j}(\theta)-Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\|X\|_{2}^{2}\right]\cdot\tau\sqrt{d/n}N^{-1}.

In Condition (A2), we consider the target covariate to be bounded for each component, thus we obtain that:

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(Z_{j}(\theta)-Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d^{3}/n}N^{-1}.

Together with (
205
), we obtain that, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, with probability at least
1-N^{-c_{1}d}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}(\theta)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}^{\epsilon}}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(Z_{j}(\theta)-Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j}(\theta^{\tau})\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C\tau\left(\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}}{n\cdot N^{2}}}\right).

Since
N\geq\log N
, it follows from (
204
) that

\left\|\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}.

Since it holds for any
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, we establish that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\left\|\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta)-\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla S(\theta)-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}.

D.12
Proof of Lemma
14

Recall that
\widehat{\phi}(\theta,\gamma)=\theta^{\intercal}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\gamma+\widehat{S}(\theta)
, then
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
, defined in (
37
), shall be expressed as

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\widehat{\phi}(\theta,{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}).

To control the distance of
\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
, we first define an intermediate estimator
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}
as follows

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\widehat{\phi}(\theta,{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}).

(206)

where
\Theta_{B}
is defined in (
51
).
In the following, we will establish the convergence rate of
\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
, and then we show that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}=\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
to complete the proof.

The optimality condition of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}
in (
206
) implies that

\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}+\nabla\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}),\theta-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\right\rangle\geq 0,\quad\text{for all}\quad\theta\in\Theta_{B}.

We substitute
\theta
with
\theta^{*}
in the above inequality:

\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}+\nabla\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}),\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\right\rangle\geq 0.

(207)

The optimality condition of
\theta^{*}
in (
43
) implies

\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}+\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})=0.

Together with (
207
), we have

\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}+\nabla\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*}),\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\right\rangle\geq 0.

Moreover, since

\nabla\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})=\nabla\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})+\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*}),

the above inequality further implies

\left\langle\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*}),{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*}+\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*}),\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\right\rangle

Note that on the event
\mathcal{G}
, we have

\left|\langle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\mathcal{U}\gamma^{*},\frac{\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}}{\|\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\|_{2}}\rangle\right|\leq\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+Ct\sqrt{d/n},

and

\left|\langle\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*}),\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\rangle\right|\leq C\sqrt{d\log N/N}\|\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\|_{2}.

Therefore, we show that

\left\langle\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*}),{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\right\rangle\leq\left(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\|{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+Ct\sqrt{d/n}+C\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)\cdot\|\theta^{*}-{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\|_{2}.

(208)

For the left-hand side of the above inequality, by the mean value theorem, there exists
\nu\in[0,1]
such that

\left\langle\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*}),{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\right\rangle=\left({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\right)^{\intercal}\widehat{H}\left((1-\nu){\theta}^{*}+\nu{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\right)\left({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\right).

On the event
\mathcal{G}
, Lemma
4
implies the positive definiteness of
\widehat{H}(\theta)
for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, then we establish the following lower bound,

\left\langle\widehat{S}({\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]})-\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*}),{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\right\rangle\gtrsim\|{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

Together with (
208
), and the bounded assumption
\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}
in Condition (A4), we have shown that

\displaystyle\|{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log(N)/N}

(209)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n},

where the second inequality holds as
N\gg n\log n
in Condition (A2) and
t>c_{0}
in Condition (A1).
It follows that

\displaystyle\|{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\|{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}-{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d}\left(\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n}\right).

When the value
t
satisfies (
18
), we apply the conditions of
n,N
in Condition (A2) to obtain that: there exists a positive constant
C>0
, such that if
C\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}<1
, then

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}-\theta^{*}\|_{1}<1.

(210)

In the following, we show that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}=\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
by the argument of contradiction.

Suppose that
{\widehat{\theta}}_{B}^{[m]}\neq\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
, for any
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, Lemma
4
implies the positive definiteness of the Hessian matrix for
\widehat{\phi}(\theta,\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]})
. Therefore,
\widehat{\phi}(\theta,\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]})
is strongly convex towards
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, and we must have
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\notin\Theta_{B}
. By the definition of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
, it holds that
\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})\leq\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})
.
Then, by the convexity of
\widehat{\phi}(\theta,{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})
for
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}
, for any
\nu\in[0,1]
,

\widehat{\phi}(\nu\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}+(1-\nu)\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B},\;{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})\leq\nu\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})+(1-\nu)\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})\leq\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}).

Note that the pathway
\nu\to\nu\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}+(1-\nu)\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}
intersects
\Theta_{B}
at a point, denoted as
\theta^{\rm inter}
. Together with the above inequality, we have
\widehat{\phi}(\theta^{\rm inter},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})\leq\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B},{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})
and
\theta^{\rm inter}\in\Theta_{B}
. Therefore, we establish that

\theta^{\rm inter}\in\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Theta_{B}}\widehat{\phi}(\theta,{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}),\quad\textrm{and}\quad\|\theta^{\rm inter}-\theta^{*}\|_{1}=1.

By the strong convexity of
\widehat{\phi}(\theta,{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}})
for
\theta\in\Theta_{B}
, we further obtain that
\theta^{\rm inter}=\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}
, which implies that

\|\theta^{[m]}_{B}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}=1.

It contradicts (
210
), therefore we show that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}_{B}=\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
. Then together with (
209
), we complete the proof.

D.13
Proof of Lemma
13

To control the probability of the event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we shall study the terms
\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}
, and
\|n(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
. For the term
\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}
, if we verify that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, we can control
\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}
using the remainder’s upper bound (
125
) in Proposition
6
, such that: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}
,

\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau^{2}\frac{d}{n}.

As for
\|n(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
, we now introduce the following lemma to establish its upper bound.
The proof is provided in Section
D.21
.

Lemma 22
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold, and the value
\tau
defined in (
31
) satisfies
\tau\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{2}}
. Then for any index
m
whose corresponding
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
with
\Theta_{\rm loc}
defined in (
120
), we have that on the event
\mathcal{G}_{3}
,

\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+\sqrt{d\log N/N},

where
\mathcal{G}_{3}
is defined in (
54
).

This lemma implies that, if we verify
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, then we can control
\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
via
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
.
Therefore, the following proof is outlined as follows:

Step 1.

Establish the upper bound of
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
, and justify that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, with high probability.

Step 2.

Apply (
125
) in Proposition
6
and Lemma
22
to control
\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\right\|_{2}
and
