\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
, and then finish the proof.

D.13.1
Proof for Step 1

We set the value
t
as
t=\sqrt{\log n}
in this proof. Then
\tau
defined in (
31
) admits the form:

\tau:=C\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)t=C\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{\log n},

and

\Theta_{\rm loc}
defined in (
120
) shall be expressed as:

\Theta_{\rm loc}:=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}:\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{d\log n/n}\right\}.

(211)

We now present the following high-probability event to control the rate of
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
.

\mathcal{E}_{4}:=\left\{\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{3/4}\right\},

(212)

On the right-hand side of the inequality of the event
\mathcal{E}_{4}
, the first term corresponds to the upper bound for
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}
, which is controlled via Theorem
6
, while the second term corresponds to the upper bound for
\|\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
that is established in Theorem
5
, with the value
t=\sqrt{\log n}
.

The following lemma formally justifies that
\mathcal{E}_{4}
happens with high probability, whose proof is provided in Section
D.22
.

Lemma 23
.

Suppose Conditions
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold, and
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\sqrt{\log n}(d/n)^{1/4}
. Then

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})\geq 1-\alpha_{0},

where
\alpha_{0}\in(0,0.01]
is the pre-specified constant used to construct
\mathcal{M}
in (
36
).

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{4}
, with
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
and a sufficiently large resampling size
M
such that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})
, it holds that:

\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\cdot n^{-3/4}\ll n^{-1/2}.

Combined with Lemma
14
, which provides an upper bound on
\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
via
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
, we obtain that on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
,

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\log n/n}+\sqrt{\log N/N}\lesssim\sqrt{\log n/n},

where the last inequality holds as
N\gg n\log N
by Condition (A2).
Therefore, we conclude that on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, with
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
and a sufficiently large resampling size
M
such that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})
, it follows from (
211
) that

\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}.

D.13.2
Proof for Step 2

Provided that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
as established in the step 1,
we now turn to establishing upper bounds for the two quantities:
\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
and
\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}
. Particularly, we apply Lemma
22
to obtain that, on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, with
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
and a sufficiently large resampling size
M
such that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})
,

\displaystyle\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+\sqrt{\log N/N}.

Using the remainder’s upper bound (
125
) in Proposition
6
, we control
\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}
in the decomposition of
\widehat{\theta}^{[m^{*}]}-\theta^{*}
. We obtain that on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, when
t=\sqrt{\log n}
,

\left\|{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log n\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\frac{\log n}{n}.

As we consider
n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}\in[c_{1},c_{2}]
in Condition (A5), the above results yield the following inequalities: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

(213)

\displaystyle\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log n\log N}{N}}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}.

Next, we plug the upper bound of
\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
in
\mathcal{E}_{4}
into (
213
). On the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, we establish that:

\displaystyle\frac{\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m^{*}]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}}{n\cdot{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\cdot n^{-3/4}+\frac{n}{N}+N^{-1/2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{n,M}.

and

\displaystyle\frac{\|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\rm Rem}^{[m^{*}]}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n{\bf V}^{*}_{1,1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left({{\rm err}_{n}(M)}+\sqrt{\frac{n\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\cdot n^{-1/4}

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log n\log N}{N}}

\displaystyle=\eta_{n,M}.

Therefore, it follows from the preceding discussions that

\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{3},

provided that
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\max\{\sqrt{n\log N/N},\;\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-1/8}\}
, and a sufficiently large resampling size
M
such that
{\rm err}_{n}(M)\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})
.
And it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{G}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4})=1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}^{c}\cup\mathcal{G}_{4}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{4}^{c})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}^{c})-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{4}^{c})-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4}^{c})=\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{4})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})-2.

By taking the liminf operators w.r.t
M\to\infty
then
n\to\infty
on both sides, we have

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3})\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\left\{\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{4})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})\right\}-2.

Combined with Lemmas
3
,
12
, and
23
, which lower bounds
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G})
,
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{4})
, and
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})
, respectively, we complete the proof.

D.14
Proof of Lemma
15

It follows Lemma
9
and Theorem
5
that on the event
\mathcal{G}
,

\displaystyle\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+\|\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

(214)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\kappa_{2}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}.

The next Lemma upper bounds
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}
, with proof provided in Section
D.23
.

Lemma 24
.

Suppose Condition
(A5)
holds. Then on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, defined in (
117
), we have

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

Combining (
214
) and the above Lemma
24
, we have obtained that on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}\sqrt{d/n}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}\right)+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}.

Moreover, as we consider
N\gg n\log N
, we shall further simplify the preceding result and complete the proof.

D.15
Proof of Lemma
16

It follows from (
135
) that,
on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, when
t\ll\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})(n/d)^{1/8}
,

\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc},\quad\textrm{for all $m\in\mathcal{M}$}.

Then we apply Lemma
22
to establish that

\displaystyle\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+N^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2},

where the second inequality holds since
N\geq n
and
\max_{l\in[L]}\|n_{l}\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}\leq 1/3
on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
.
Together with Lemma
15
where we establish the upper bound for
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}
, we further obtain that: on the event
\mathcal{G}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\frac{z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\sqrt{d/n}+\tau^{2}(d/n)^{3/4}.

Since
\tau\asymp(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})})t\ll(n/d)^{1/8}
,
we further simplify the preceding inequality, such that

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}\leq C,

for some positive constant
C>0
.

D.16
Proof of Lemma
17

It follows from the definition of
\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*}),\nabla S(\theta^{*})
that

\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}p(X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j},\theta^{*})\otimes X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left[p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X\right].

Since
\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\}_{j\in[N]}
are i.i.d. drawn from
\mathbb{Q}_{X}
, by the classic CLT, we have

\sqrt{N}\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right)\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{dK},\;{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}\left(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X\right)\right).

Note that
(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\gamma^{*}_{l}(\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}-\mu^{(l)})
.
According to the limiting distribution of
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
in Condition (A5), we establish that

\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\gamma_{l}^{*}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right]^{-1/2}(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0_{dK},{\bf I}_{dK}).

Combining the above two results, we show that

(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{dK},\;\sum_{l=1}^{L}\gamma_{l}^{*}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}+\frac{1}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X)\right).

By the expression of
W^{*}
in (
124
), it implies that

(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0_{dK},W^{*}).

By Slutsky’ theorem, we further establish that

\mathcal{D}^{*}=-[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\left[(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}+\nabla\widehat{S}(\theta^{*})-\nabla S(\theta^{*})\right]\xlongrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}W^{*}[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}).

D.17
Proof of Lemma
18

We consider that
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
with
\Theta_{\rm loc}
defined in (
120
). That is,

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}.

Since
\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}=\nu\theta^{*}+(1-\nu)\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}
for some
\nu\in[0,1]
, it holds that

\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n},

(215)

as well, which implies that

\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{d}\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d^{2}/n}\leq 1,

where the last inequality holds if
\tau\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{2}}
. Therefore, we have
\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{B}
, where the bounded parameter set
\Theta_{B}
is defined in (
51
). Then it follows from Lemma
1
that
\lambda_{\max}\left(\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\right)\leq C
, for some constant
C>0
.

Regarding the term
\text{Rem}_{1}^{[m]}
, on the event
\mathcal{G}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\text{Rem}_{1}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\right)\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\|_{2}\|{\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

(216)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}+t\sqrt{d/n})\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2},

where the last inequality holds as we consider
\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq C
in Condition (A4) and
t\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{3}}
as specified in (
18
).
Regarding the term
\text{Rem}_{2}^{[m]}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\text{Rem}_{2}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}\right)\left\|\left(\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left(\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}(\theta^{*})\right)-\left(\nabla\widehat{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-\nabla{S}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\right)\right\|_{2}

Since
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, we apply Lemma
12
to establish that on the event
\mathcal{G}_{4}
:

\left\|\text{Rem}_{2}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log N}{n\cdot N}}\cdot\tau.

(217)

With respect to the term
\text{Rem}_{3}^{[m]}
, the following inequality holds:

\left\|\text{Rem}_{3}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}({\theta}^{*})+(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right\|_{2}.

By Lemma
2
which establishes the continuity property of
H(\cdot)
, we have

\left\|\left[H\left(\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}\right)\right]^{-1}-\left[H(\theta^{*})\right]^{-1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n},

where the second inequality follows from (
215
).
Moreover, on the event
\mathcal{G}
, by its definition in (
54
), we have

\left\|\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}({\theta}^{*})+(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U})\gamma^{*}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\widehat{S}({\theta}^{*})-\nabla{S}({\theta}^{*})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{d\log N/N}+t\sqrt{d/n}.

The combination of the above three inequalities leads to

\left\|\text{Rem}_{3}^{[m]}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau\cdot t\cdot\frac{d}{n}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}\log N}{n\cdot N}}+\tau^{2}\frac{d}{n},

(218)

where the second inequality holds as
\tau:=C(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})})t\gtrsim t
.
Now, we combine (
216
), (
217
), and (
218
) to complete the proof.

D.18
Proof of Lemma
19

We consider that
h(X)\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
is a subgaussian random vector, then its
k
-entry
h_{k}(X)\in\mathbb{R}
is a subgaussian random variable for any
k=1,...,K.

In the following discussions, we first study the concentration inequality for
h_{k}(X)\cdot X
for each
k=1,...,K
. Then we will move on studying
h(X)\otimes X
.

For any
k=1,...,K
, we denote
h_{k}(X)
’s subgaussian norm as
\|h_{k}(X)\|_{\psi_{2}}
. Since
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a subgaussian random vector with subgaussian norm
\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}
, their product
Z=h_{k}(X)\cdot X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a subexponential random vector with subexponential norm:

\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}=\|h_{k}(X)\cdot X\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|h_{k}(X)\|_{\psi_{2}}\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}.

Notice that the
\ell_{2}
norm and the subgaussian norm are equivalent up to constants; therefore, we establish that

\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq c\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}.

(219)

The last equality holds since we consider
\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}
is upper bounded by some constant, implied by Condition (A1).
We have
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
i.i.d. samples of
X
, and for each
i
, we denote
Z_{i}=h_{k}(X_{i})\cdot X_{i}
. Clearly,
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
are i.i.d. samples of
Z
.
Our goal is to bound
\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}
with high probability.

For any fixed unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define the projection of the error along
u
as follows:

S_{u}:=u^{\intercal}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u^{\intercal}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[u^{\intercal}Z]\right).

(220)

It follows from (
219
) that
u^{\intercal}Z_{i}
is a sub-exponential random scalar with subexponential norm with
\|u^{\intercal}Z_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}=\|Z_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}
. Then the Bernstein inequality for subexponential random variables implies that for any
t>0
:

\mathbb{P}\Biggl(\Bigl|S_{u}\Bigr|\geq t\Biggr)\leq 2\exp\left[-cn\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}},\frac{t}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right\}\right],

(221)

where
c>0
is an absolute constant.

We now wish to obtain a bound on the full
\ell_{2}
error:

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}=\sup_{\|u\|_{2}=1}\left|u^{\intercal}\Bigl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\Bigr)\right|=\sup_{\|u\|_{2}=1}|S_{u}|.

We define a
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. It follows from Corollary 4.2.13 of
Vershynin 2018
that the cardinality
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|
satisfies

|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq(1+2/\epsilon)^{d}.

For a fixed
u\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}
, we already have the inequality established in (
221
).
A union bound over all
u\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
yields that, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}|S_{u}|<t\right\}\geq 1-2\left(1+\frac{2}{\epsilon}\right)^{d}\exp\left[-cn\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}},\frac{t}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right\}\right].

(222)

Next we pass from maximum over the net to the supreme over the whole sphere. By the definition of
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
, we know that for every
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there must exist a
v\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\|u-v\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
. Then, for any fixed vector
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we pick the net point
v\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
, then by the triangle inequality, we establish the upper bound of
|S_{u}|
, defined in (
220
), as follows:

|S_{u}|\leq|S_{v}|+|S_{u-v}|.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

|S_{u-v}|=\left|(u-v)^{\intercal}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right)\right|\leq\|u-v\|_{2}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right|_{2}\leq\epsilon\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}.

Thus, we obtain that:

|S_{u}|\leq|S_{v}|+\epsilon\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}.

Since this is true for every
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we can take the supremum over
u
:

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|S_{u}|\leq\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}|S_{v}|+\epsilon\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}.

Rearranging the above inequality, we shall obtain that

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}|S_{v}|.

Together with the result in (
222
), we establish that for any
t>0
:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}<\frac{t}{1-\varepsilon}\right\}\geq 1-2\exp\left[-c\cdot n\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}},\frac{t}{\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}}\right\}+d\log\left(1+\frac{2}{\epsilon}\right)\right].

We pick
t=a\cdot\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}
, and
\epsilon=1/2
. Together with
n\geq d
, the above inequality becomes:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}<2a\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\sqrt{d/n}\right\}\geq 1-2\exp\left[-c\cdot da^{2}+d\log 2\right].

Therefore, as long as the value of
a
satisfies that
a^{2}>c\cdot\log 2
for some constant
c>0
, we establish that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z]\right\|_{2}\leq 2a\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\sqrt{d/n}\right\}\geq 1-2\exp(-ca^{2}).

Since

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})\otimes X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[h(X)\otimes X]\right\|_{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{k}(X_{i})\cdot X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[h_{k}(X)\cdot X]\right\|_{2},

we further establish that: for any
a>c_{0}
with
c_{0}
being some positive constant,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})\otimes X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[h(X)\otimes X]\right\|_{2}<a\cdot\max_{k=1...,K}\|h_{k}\|_{\ell_{2}}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}\right\}\geq 1-2\exp(-ca^{2}).

D.19
Proof of Lemma
20

We now control remainder terms in order.

Control of
{\rm Rem}_{1}^{(l)}
.

Since
\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)})
is bounded in Condition (B1), and
\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)})-f^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)})\in[-1,1]
, for each category
k=1,...,K
, we have
\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))
as a subgaussian random variable with

\max_{k=1,...,K}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))\right]^{2}\leq C\max_{k=1,...,K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}.

Then we apply Lemma
19
to obtain that: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
:

\|{\rm Rem}_{1}^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim\max_{k=1,...K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}t\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

(223)

Control of
{\rm Rem}_{2}^{(l)}
.

Similarly, we shall control
{\rm Rem}_{2}^{(l)}
as follows: for any value
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\|{\rm Rem}_{2}^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim\max_{k=1,...,K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{Q})}t\cdot\sqrt{d/N}.

(224)

Additionally, since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[(\omega^{(l)}(X))^{2}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X))^{4}]\right)^{1/2},

where the last inequality follows from the bounded assumption of
\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)
in Condition (B1). Therefore, we establish that

\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{Q})}\lesssim\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{c}-f^{(l)}_{c}\right\|_{\ell_{4}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}.

Note
\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)})-f^{(l)}_{k}(X^{(l)})\in[-1,1]
is also a subgaussian random variable with
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{4}
norms differing only by a constant. Therefore,

\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{Q})}\lesssim\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}.

Then putting the above result back to (
224
), we establish that: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\|{\rm Rem}_{2}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\max_{k=1,...,K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\sqrt{d/N}.

(225)

Control of
{\rm Rem}_{3}^{(l)}
.

{\rm Rem}_{3}^{(l)}
admits the following form

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{3}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\omega(X^{(l)}_{i})\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}{\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}-1\right)\cdot\left(f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-{\bf y}\right)\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot\left(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y}\right)\otimes X\right],

where the second term on the right-hand side satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot\left(f^{(l)}(X)-{\bf y}\right)\otimes X\right]=0,

since for each category
k=1,...,K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot{\bf 1}(Y=k)\cdot X\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{Y|X}}\left({\bf 1}(Y=k)|X\right)\cdot X\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\cdot X\right].

It follows from the bounded assumption of
\omega^{(l)}(\cdot)
in Condition (B1), and
f^{(l)}_{k}\in[-1,1]
, that

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\right]^{2}\leq C\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right]^{2}=C\cdot\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}^{2}.

Then we apply Lemma
19
to obtain that: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
,

\|{\rm Rem}_{3}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\sqrt{d/n}.

(226)

Control of
{\rm Rem}_{4}^{(l)}
.

We shall decompose
{\rm Rem}_{4}^{(l)}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{4}^{(l)}

\displaystyle={\rm Rem}_{41}^{(l)}+{\rm Rem}_{42}^{(l)},\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{with}

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{41}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}{\omega^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})}-1\right)\cdot\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})-f^{(l)}(X^{(l)}_{i})\right)\right]\otimes X^{(l)}_{i}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-f^{(l)}(X)\right)\otimes X\right],

\displaystyle{\rm Rem}_{42}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}}\left[\omega^{(l)}(X)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)}{\omega^{(l)}(X)}-1\right)\cdot\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}(X)-f^{(l)}(X)\right)\otimes X\right].

We apply Lemma
19
to establish: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
:

\|{\rm Rem}_{41}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq t\cdot\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot\max_{k=1...,K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

For
{\rm Rem}_{42}^{(l)}
,
we observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)-\omega^{(l)}(X)\right)\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\right)X\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left[\left|\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)-\omega^{(l)}(X)\right)\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\right)\right|\cdot\left\|X\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}\left|\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}(X)-\omega^{(l)}(X)\right)\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}(X)-f^{(l)}_{k}(X)\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}}[\|X\|_{2}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle({\rm Cauchy-Schwarz})

\displaystyle=C\sqrt{d}\left\|\left(\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}-\omega^{(l)}\right)\left(\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right)\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}

\displaystyle(\textrm{Bounded $\ell_{2}$ norm of $\|X\|_{2}$})

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}\left\|\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}-\omega^{(l)}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{d}\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}

\displaystyle(\textrm{as $\omega(\cdot)$ is bounded}).

We combine the above results to conclude that: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\|{\rm Rem}_{4}^{(l)}\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle t\cdot\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot\max_{k=1...,K}\left\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}_{k}-f^{(l)}_{k}\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{d/n}).

(227)

Proof of equation (
151
).

We combine (
223
),(
225
),(
226
), and (
227
) to establish that: with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\cdot\left(\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}+\max_{k=1,...,K}\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}-f^{(l)}\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\right)\sqrt{d/n}

\displaystyle+t\cdot\left\|\frac{\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}}{\omega^{(l)}}-1\right\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot\max_{k=1,...,K}\|\widehat{f}^{(l)}-f^{(l)}\|_{\ell_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{(l)})}\cdot\sqrt{d}.

Next, we apply Condition (B2) to further establish that: for any
t\geq c_{0}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-c_{1}t^{2}}-\delta_{n}
:

\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{4}{\rm Rem}_{j}^{(l)}\right\|_{2}\leq t\cdot(\eta_{\omega}+\eta_{f})\sqrt{d/n}+t\cdot\eta_{\omega}\eta_{f}\sqrt{d},

where
\delta_{n}\to 0
is a vanishing sequence.

D.20
Proof of Lemma
21

The proof of the lemma relies on the following result of handling the Kronecker product: for a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
,
B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},
and
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K}
,
we have

(A\otimes B){\rm vec}(V)={\rm vec}(BVA^{\intercal}).

(228)

We apply (
228
) and obtain

\displaystyle\left(A\otimes XX^{\intercal}\right){\rm vec}(V)={\rm vec}(XX^{\intercal}VA).

Then we have

\displaystyle v^{\intercal}\left(A\otimes XX^{\intercal}\right)v

\displaystyle=[{\rm vec}(V)]^{\intercal}\left(A\otimes XX^{\intercal}\right){\rm vec}(V)

\displaystyle=[{\rm vec}(V)]^{\intercal}{\rm vec}(XX^{\intercal}VA)={\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VA),

which completes the proof of (
157
). By the von Neumann trace inequality, we have

\left|{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}VA)\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\min(K,p)}\sigma_{k}\left(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V\right)\cdot\sigma_{k}(A)\leq\sigma_{1}(A)\cdot{\rm Tr}(V^{\intercal}XX^{\intercal}V).

D.21
Proof of Lemma
22

We first study the term
W^{*}
. For clarity, we consider the case
n_{l}=n
for all
l\in[L]
, without loss of generality. Then

n\cdot W^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\gamma_{l}^{*})^{2}(n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})+\frac{n}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X).

It follows from the triangle inequality that:

\|n\cdot W^{*}\|_{2}\leq\max_{l\in[L]}\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X)\|_{2}.

Since each entry of the probability vector
p(X^{\mathbb{Q}},\theta^{*})
is bounded, together with Condition (A2) where
X^{\mathbb{Q}}
is a bounded random vector, it follows that

\|{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{X}}(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X)\|_{2}
is bounded as well when we consider the fixed dimension setting. Together with Condition (A5) that
\|n{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}
is upper bounded, we have

\|n\cdot W^{*}\|_{2}\leq C_{1}+\frac{n}{N}C_{2}\leq C,

where
C_{1},C_{2},C>0
denote some positive constants.

By the expressions of
\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}
and
{\bf V}^{*}
, the error of
\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}
admits the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|n\cdot(\widehat{W}^{[m]}-W^{*})\|_{2}+C_{2}\left\|\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-H(\theta^{*})\right\|_{2}

(229)

\displaystyle+C_{3}\left\|\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-H(\theta^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
are some positive constants depending on
\|[H(\theta^{*})]^{-1}\|_{2}
,
\|nW^{*}\|_{2}
.

We first study the term
\|\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-H(\theta^{*})\|_{2}
. If
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
, i.e.,

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d/n}.

we have

\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau\sqrt{d^{2}/n}<1
, where the last inequality holds as
\tau\lesssim\sqrt{n/d^{2}}
. Therefore,
\widehat{\theta}^{[m]}\in\Theta_{B}
. We apply event
\mathcal{G}_{3}
to study the term
\left\|\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-H(\theta^{*})\right\|_{2}
.
On the event
\mathcal{G}_{3}
,

\left\|\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})-H(\theta^{*})\right\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{d\log N/N}.

Next, we study the term
\|n\cdot(\widehat{W}^{[m]}-W^{*})\|_{2}
. By the expressions of
\widehat{W}^{[m]}
and
W^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{W}^{[m]}-W^{*}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}_{l})^{2}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\gamma^{*}_{l})^{2}{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right\}+\left\{\frac{1}{N}\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p(X,\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\otimes X\right)-\frac{1}{N}{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X\right)\right\}

\displaystyle:=T_{1}+T_{2}.

For the term
T_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle n\cdot T_{1}=\sum_{l=1}^{L}(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}_{l})^{2}\left[n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\right]+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left[(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}_{l})^{2}-(\gamma^{*}_{l})^{2}\right]\cdot\left[n\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right].

Since
\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]},\gamma^{*}\in\Delta^{L}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left[(\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}_{l})^{2}-(\gamma^{*}_{l})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\gamma^{*}\|_{2}^{2}=\langle\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}+\gamma^{*},\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}+\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\leq 2\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

Therefore, we establish that

\|n\cdot T_{1}\|_{2}\leq\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+2\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}.

For the term
T_{2}
,
since
X^{\mathbb{Q}}
is a bounded random vector, there exists a positive constant
C>0
such that

\widehat{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p(X,\widehat{\theta}^{[m]})\otimes X\right)\leq C,\quad{\bf V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p(X,\theta^{*})\otimes X\right)\leq C.

Therefore,

\|N\cdot T_{2}\|_{2}\leq C.

Consequently, we establish that

\|n\cdot(\widehat{W}^{[m]}-W^{*})\|_{2}\leq\|n\cdot T_{1}\|_{2}+\|n\cdot T_{2}\|_{2}\leq\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+2\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+C\cdot\frac{n}{N}.

Putting the above results back to (
229
), we establish that:

\displaystyle\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}^{[m]}-{\bf V}^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+\sqrt{d\log N/N}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot(\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}-{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}+\frac{n}{N}+\sqrt{d\log N/N},

where the last inequality holds as
\max_{l\in[L]}\|n\cdot{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}\|_{2}\leq C
for some constant
C>0
as assumed in Condition (A5).

D.22
Proof of Lemma
23

We denote the following events to facilitate discussions.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right)\right\}

(230)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{6}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\|\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n{\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{3/4}}\right\}

For the event
\mathcal{E}_{5}
, it follows from Theorem
6
that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\liminf_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5})\geq 1-\alpha_{0}.

Regarding the event
\mathcal{E}_{6}
, we apply Theorem
5
with
t=\sqrt{\log n}
to establish that:
if
\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})\gg\sqrt{\log n}(d/n)^{1/4}
, then there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
such that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6})\geq 1-n^{-c_{1}d}-\delta_{n},

where the sequence
\delta_{n}\to 0
is given in Condition (A1). Then with
n\to\infty
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6})=1.

By the triangle inequality, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\gamma}^{[m^{*}]}-\gamma^{*}_{\rm ap}\|_{2}+\|\gamma_{\rm ap}^{*}-\gamma^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\left(\frac{{\rm err}_{n}(M)}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}(\mathcal{U})}\right)^{2}\log n\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{3/4}.

That is,
\mathcal{E}_{4}\supseteq\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6})=1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{6}^{c})\geq 1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5}^{c})-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6}^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6})-1.

We combine the above results to obtain that:

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4})\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left[\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5})+\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6})-1\right]\geq 1-\alpha_{0}.

D.23
Proof of Lemma
24

For any
m\in\mathcal{M}
, by the definition of
\mathcal{M}
, we have

\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}\leq 1.1\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}.

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, specified in (
117
), we have

\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{|\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}-\mu^{(l)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}\leq 1.05\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}.

By the triangle inequality, the above two inequalities imply that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[\widehat{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+1/n_{l}}}\leq 2.15\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}.

Then on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we establish that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{[{\bf V}^{(l)}_{\mu}]_{j,j}+\frac{4}{3}n_{l}}}\leq 2.15\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/(dKL)}.

In Condition (A5), we suppose that
n_{l}\cdot[{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}]_{j,j}\in[c_{1},c_{2}]
for all
l\in[L]
and
j\in[dK]
, with two positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Thus, we obtain that

\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}\right|\leq C\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}\cdot n^{-1/2},

which further implies that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{F}=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{dK}\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{dKL}\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\max_{1\leq j\leq dK}\left|\widehat{\mu}^{(l,m)}_{j}-\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}_{j}\right|\leq C\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

The above inequality holds for any
m\in\mathcal{M}
, resulting in

\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}^{[m]}-\mathcal{U}\|_{2}\leq C\cdot z_{\alpha_{0}/dKL}\cdot\sqrt{d/n}.

Appendix E
Experimental Setups and Additional Experiments

E.1
Setups for Figures
2
and
4

In this subsection, we present the detailed setups for the experiments in Figures
2
and
4
. All experiments involve generating labeled data from
L
source domains and unlabeled data from the target domain. For each source domain
l\in[L]
, the labeled data
\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{i\in[n_{l}]}
are i.i.d. generated as follows:

•

Covariates
X^{(l)}_{i}\sim\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}
.

•

Labels
Y^{(l)}_{i}
follows a multinomial distribution with
K+1
categories, where

\mathbb{P}^{(l)}(Y=c|X)=\frac{\exp(X^{\intercal}\beta^{(l)}_{c})}{\sum_{k=0}^{K}\exp(X^{\intercal}\beta_{k}^{(l)})},\quad\textrm{for $c=0,1,...,K$.}

For the target domain, the unlabeled data
\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{j}\}_{j\in[N]}
are i.i.d. drawn from
\mathbb{Q}_{X}
. We will specify the parameters
L,K,\{n_{l}\},N,\{\beta^{(l)}_{c}\}_{l,c}
and distribution
\{\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}\}_{L},\mathbb{Q}_{X}
across settings.

Setup for Figure
2
.

We consider a binary classification task
(K=1)
with
L=2
source domains under the covariate shift regime. Specifically, we set the source covariate distributions as
\mathbb{P}^{(1)}_{X}=\mathbb{P}^{(2)}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0_{4},\mathbf{I}_{4})
, while the target covariate distribution is
\mathbb{Q}_{X}=\mathcal{N}((-1,-1,1,1)^{\intercal},\mathbf{I}_{4})
. The class-conditional coefficients are defined as

\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=0}=0_{4},\;\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=1}=(-0.4,-0.4,0.2,0.2)^{\intercal},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=0}=0_{4},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=1}=(1,1,0.2,0.2)^{\intercal}.

The total number of labeled source samples is fixed at
n_{1}+n_{2}=4,000
, while the proportion from the first source varies over
\{0.1,0.2,\ldots,0.9\}
, as depicted on the horizontal axis of Figure
2
. The number of unlabeled target samples is set to
N=10{,}000
.

Next, we detail the implementation of each method evaluated in Figure
2
. The proposed method is implemented via Algorithm
2
, where we use logistic regression to estimate both nuisance components
\widehat{f}^{(l)}
and
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}
for each source
l=1,2
. For the
ERM
baseline, we pool the source data and fit a single logistic regression model across the combined dataset. The Group DRO baseline is implemented using the Mirror Prox algorithm as described in Algorithm
4
.

To compute the
non-reducible loss
for each source domain, we proceed as follows. For the first source, given the target covariates
\{X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
, we simulate binary outcomes
\{Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
from the distribution
\mathbb{P}^{(1)}(Y=1|X=X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i})
. The corresponding non-reducible loss is then computed as:

-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{c=0}^{1}\mathbf{1}(Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}=c)\log\mathbb{P}^{(1)}(Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}=c\mid X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i}).

Similarly, to compute the non-reducible loss for the second source, we simulate
\{Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
using
\mathbb{P}^{(2)}(Y=1|X=X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i})
, and evaluate the loss via:

-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{c=0}^{1}\mathbf{1}(Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}=c)\log\mathbb{P}^{(2)}(Y_{i}^{\mathbb{Q}}=c\mid X^{\mathbb{Q}}_{i}).

Setup for Figure
4
.
We describe the settings used to illustrate the
nonregular
,
unstable
, and
regular
regimes in Figure
4
. All settings involve binary classification (K = 1) under covariate shift. In particular, we set the covariate distributions
\mathbb{P}^{(l)}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0_{d},{\bf I}_{d})
, for
l\in[L]
, and
\mathbb{Q}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0.1_{d},{\bf I}_{d})
. The source sample size is fixed at
n_{l}=400
, for
l\in[L]
, while the target sample size is fixed at
N=4000
.

•

Nonregular.
We set
L=2
and
d=20
. The class-conditional coefficients are:

\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=0}=0_{d},\;\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=1}=(6,0.5_{3},0_{d-4})^{\intercal},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=0}=0_{d},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=1}=(0.5_{4},0_{d-4})^{\intercal}.

•

Unstable.
We set
L=4
and
d=4
. The class-conditional coefficients are: for
l\in[L]
,

\beta^{(l)}_{c=0}=0_{d},\;\beta^{(l)}_{c=1}=(1,1,0,0)^{\intercal}+U^{(l)},\;\textrm{with }U^{(l)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0_{d},0.01{\bf I}_{d}).

•

Regular.
We set
L=2
and
d=20
. The class-conditional coefficients are:

\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=0}=0_{d},\;\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=1}=(0.5_{2},0_{d-2})^{\intercal},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=0}=0_{d},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=1}=(0_{2},0.5_{2},0_{d-4})^{\intercal}.

For both ERM and our proposed method (Algorithm
2
), we fit logistic regression models.

E.2
Additional Comparisons Against Group DRO

In this section, we numerically compare our proposed method implemented in Algorithm
2
, against the Group DRO approach in both regimes with and without covariate shift. The implementation of Group DRO proceeds as follows: given labeled data
\{X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n_{l}}
for each source
l=1,...,L
, Group DRO solves the following minimax optimization problem:

\widehat{\theta}_{\rm gdro}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{dK}}\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\theta,\gamma),\quad\textrm{with}\quad\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\theta,\gamma):=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\gamma_{l}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{l}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\ell(X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i},\theta),

(231)

where
\ell(x,y,\theta)
denotes the cross-entropy loss. To solve (
231
), we apply the optimistic-gradient Mirror Prox algorithm in Algorithm
4
.
As discussed in Section
3.1
of the main paper, the two-step Mirror Prox reduces oscillations near saddle points and achieves faster convergence rates in
smooth convex-concave settings.
As a remark, we also implemented the single-step gradient-based method from
Sagawa et al. 2019
. However, we found that this method often exhibits unstable convergence behavior and does not fully converge within the maximum number of iterations, even with extensive learning rate tuning in our experiments.

We now describe the experimental setup. We consider a binary classification task (
K=1
) with
L=2
source domains.
The covariate distributions for both source domains are
\mathbb{P}^{(1)}_{X}=\mathbb{P}^{(2)}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0_{4},{\bf I}_{4})
. In the no covariate shift regime, the target covariate distribution matches the source one, i.e.
\mathbb{Q}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0_{4},{\bf I}_{4})
. In the presence of covariate shift, the target distribution is shifted to
\mathcal{N}(0.5_{4},{\bf I}_{4})
.
The class-conditional coefficients are defined as

\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=0}=0_{4},\;\beta^{(l=1)}_{c=1}=(-0.4,-0.4,0.2,0.2)^{\intercal},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=0}=0_{4},\;\beta^{(l=2)}_{c=1}=(1,1,0.2,0.2)^{\intercal}.

The sample size for each source domain is
n_{1}=n_{2}=n
, where
n
varies over
\{500,1000,1500,...,5000\}
. The number of unlabeled target samples is fixed at
N=10{,}000
.

The proposed approach is implemented using Algorithm
2
, where we apply logistic regression to estimate both nuisance components
\widehat{f}^{(l)}
and
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}
for each
l=1,2
. The Group DRO method is implemented via Algorithm
4
with learning rates set as
\eta_{\theta}=0.01
and
\eta_{\gamma}=0.1
.
We evaluate both methods in terms of the estimation error
\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}
, where
\widehat{\theta}
denotes the estimated model returned by each method, and
\theta^{*}
is the population CG-DRO model, computed using Algorithm
2
on a much larger dataset with
n=100{,}000
and
N=200{,}000
. In addition, we assess both approaches by their worst-case loss, as defined in (
2
).

Data:

Source data
\{(X^{(l)}_{i},Y^{(l)}_{i})\}_{i\in[n_{l}]}
for
l\in[L]
; learning rates for Mirror Prox:
\eta_{\theta},\eta_{\gamma}
; maximum iteration
T
, tolerance
\epsilon
.

Set
\theta_{0}=\bar{\theta}_{0}=0
,
\gamma_{0}=\bar{\gamma}_{0}=(1/L,\cdots,1/L)
, and
{\rm gap}=1
;

while

0\leq t\leq T
and
{\rm diff}\geq\epsilon

do

Intermediate Step
:

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}-\eta_{\theta}\cdot\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t},\bar{\gamma}_{t}),\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;\bar{\gamma}_{t+1,l}=\frac{\gamma_{t,l}\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{\gamma}\cdot\left[\nabla_{\gamma}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t},\bar{\gamma}_{t})\right]_{l}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}\gamma_{t,j}\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{\gamma}\cdot\left[\nabla_{\gamma}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t},\bar{\gamma}_{t})\right]_{j}\right)};

Correction Step
:

\displaystyle{\theta}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}-\eta_{\theta}\cdot\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t+1},\bar{\gamma}_{t+1}),\;\;\textrm{and}\;\;{\gamma}_{t+1,l}=\frac{\gamma_{t,l}\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{\gamma}\cdot\left[\nabla_{\gamma}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t+1},\bar{\gamma}_{t+1})\right]_{l}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}\gamma_{t,j}\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{\gamma}\cdot\left[\nabla_{\gamma}\widehat{\phi}_{\rm gdro}(\bar{\theta}_{t+1},\bar{\gamma}_{t+1})\right]_{j}\right)};

Set
\widehat{\theta}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\bar{\theta}_{s}
, and
\widehat{\gamma}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\bar{\gamma}_{s}
.

Evaluate the duality gap:
{\rm gap}=\max_{\gamma\in\Delta^{L}}\widehat{\phi}(\widehat{\theta},\gamma)-\min_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{Kd}}\widehat{\phi}(\theta,\widehat{\gamma})
.
end while

Result:

Mirror Prox estimators
\widehat{\theta}_{\rm gdro}
,
\widehat{\gamma}_{\rm gdro}
.

Algorithm 4

Mirror Prox Algorithm for Group DRO

Figures
10
and
9
compare the performance of the proposed method and Group DRO under the covariate shift and no covariate shift regimes, respectively. As shown in Figure
9
, both methods exhibit nearly identical estimation error and worst-case loss, and both achieve decreasing distance to
\theta^{*}
as the sample size increases. This observation confirms that the two approaches align in the non-shift regime, consistent with the theoretical discussion in Section
2.1
. In contrast, under covariate shift (Figure
10
), the two methods differ from each other, and Group DRO has a higher worst-case loss compared to our proposed method.

, where

\theta^{*}
is the population CG-DRO model. The right panel corresponds to the worst-case loss as defined in (
2
). The sample size for each source domain is
n_{1}=n_{2}=n
, where
n
varies over
\{500,1000,1500,...,5000\}
. The target sample size is fixed at
N=10000.

:
Comparison of the proposed method and Group DRO under the covariate shift regime. The left panel displays the estimation error
|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}|_{2}
, where
\widehat{\theta}
is the estimator returned by each method, and
\theta^{*}
is the population CG-DRO model. The right panel corresponds to the worst-case loss as defined in (
2
). The sample size for each source domain is
n_{1}=n_{2}=n
, where
n
varies over

. The target sample size is fixed at
N=10000.

E.3
Additional Inference Results

In this section, we introduce a more challenging setting to validate the proposed inferential procedure.

(S5)

This setting captures a spectrum of regimes ranging from
regular
to
nonregular
, controlled by the parameter
\delta
. We consider a multi-class classification task with three categories (
K=3
) and
L=3
source domains. The target distribution is
\mathbb{Q}_{X}=\mathcal{N}(0.2_{d},0.25{\bf I}_{d})
with
d=5
. For each source
l\in[L]
and category
k\in[K]
, the function
\phi_{k}^{(l)}(\cdot)
in (
49
) is generated as follows:

\displaystyle\phi_{k}^{(l)}(X)

\displaystyle=a^{(l,k)}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}w^{(l)}_{j}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{j}-\frac{k}{4})^{2}}{4}\right)+b^{(l,k)}X_{1}^{\intercal}X_{2}+c^{(l,k)}\sin\left((X_{3}-X_{4})+\frac{k}{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\delta X_{1}{\bf 1}(l=1,k=1),

where
a^{(l,k)},b^{(l,k)},c^{(l,k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}{\rm Uniform}(-0.5,0.5)
and
w^{(l)}\sim{\rm Dirichlet}({\bf 1}_{d})
. The parameter
\delta\in\{0,0.1,...,1.0\}
perturbs the first entry of the first source’s class-1 coefficient vector. Non-regularity gets pronounced when
\delta
increases.

We implement the proposed approach using Algorithm
3
, which involves the estimation of
\widehat{\mu}^{(l)}
and
\widehat{\bf V}_{\mu}^{(l)}
, both constructed based on the nuisance components
\widehat{f}^{(l)}
and
\widehat{\omega}^{(l)}
introduced in Section
B.2
. In this experiment, we employ the XGBoost algorithm with cross-validation to estimate these quantities.

Figure
11
presents the empirical coverage and average interval lengths for various methods for
(S5)
.
Due to the computational burden of the
Bootstrap
method, which requires fitting
B=500
ML models with cross-validation per simulation, we omit it from this experiment. Aligning with patterns for the parametric settings in Figure
8
, the
Normality
method achieves nominal
95\%
coverage only in the regular regime when the perturbation level
\delta=0
, but quickly deteriorates as
\delta
increases and the problem becomes nonregular. In contrast, both the oracle
OBA
method and our
Proposed
approach maintain valid coverage uniformly across all values of
\delta
in both dimensions. Even though our method does not require access to the true parameter
\theta^{*}_{1}
, it yields average interval lengths comparable to those of the oracle
OBA
, particularly in the more nonregular settings with larger
\delta
.

Figure 11
:
Empirical coverage and average interval lengths of different inference methods under
(S5)
. The
Bootstrap
method is omitted due to its high computational cost, which requires refitting 500 ML models per round. Results are averaged over 500 simulation rounds.
